3335	
3336	Who counts as a dependant — Tax Code art. 387 par. 2, numerals 2 and 3 as amended by Law 2411 of 2024 art. 2: children up to 18; children aged 18 to 25 whose higher or technical education the taxpayer finances; children over 18 in a certified state of physical or psychological dependence; a spouse or permanent partner dependent through absence of income or annual income below 260 tax units, or through certified physical or psychological dependence; and parents and siblings on the same conditions.
3337	A benefit-stacking point. Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 3 provides that a single dependant ordinarily gives rise to only one of the two dependant deductions — "except where the taxpayer has income from an employment or statutory and regulatory relationship, in which case both deductions may be applied for the same dependant". For an employee, therefore, the engine may take both the art. 387 dependant deduction (10% of gross, capped at 32 tax units per month) and the art. 336 additional 72 tax units per dependant, for the same dependant.
3338	Legal basis: Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 55, 56, 119, 126-1, 126-4, 206 num. 10, 336 nums. 3 and 5, 383 par. 3, 387, 387-1, 388; Law 2411 of 2024 art. 2; Decree 2231 of 2023 arts. 3 and 9; DIAN Concepto 015007 int. 973 of 13 September 2023; Substantive Labour Code arts. 128, 149; Law 100 of 1993 art. 15 num. 2 (as amended by Law 797 of 2003 art. 3); Law 100 of 1993 art. 18. Decree 1625 of 2016 arts. 1.2.1.22.42, 1.2.4.1.12 and 1.2.4.1.28 (Decree 2250 of 2017 arts. 8 and 9).
3339	
3340	Module 6: Income Tax Withholding System
3341	Purpose: To define the entire income tax withholding system.
3342	Resident / non-resident treatment matrix (control B1). Built before the prose because Colombia's treatment turns on tax residence in a way that is frequently mis-configured. All amounts in COP; tax unit (UVT) 2026 = 52,374; minimum wage 2026 = 1,750,905.
3343	Income type
3344	RESIDENT
3345	NON-RESIDENT
3346	Regular salary from a Colombian employer, work performed in Colombia
3347	Progressive table, Tax Code art. 383 (0 / 19 / 28 / 33 / 35 / 37 / 39%), applied to the art. 388 depurated monthly base. Method: Procedure 1 or Procedure 2
3348	Flat 20%, Tax Code art. 408, applied to the gross payment. No art. 388 depuration, no 25% exempt-income deduction, no dependants or other art. 387 deductions, no tax-free band. Confirmed by DIAN Concepto 011108 of 29 June 2026, which upholds Concepto 1789 (int. 14595) of 2025
3349	Regular salary from a Colombian employer, work performed entirely outside Colombia
3350	Worldwide income — art. 383 progressive table applies
3351	Not taxable in Colombia — foreign-source income. No withholding. DIAN Oficio 28380 of 2019
3352	Annual / 13th-month bonus (statutory service bonus)
3353	Procedure 1: the statutory minimum bonus is withheld separately on its own read of the art. 383 table (art. 385). Procedure 2: folded into the ordinary monthly base
3354	Flat 20% under art. 408, on the gross bonus. No separate treatment and no separate table read
3355	Severance payments — statutory severance savings and interest
3356	Exempt in full where the average monthly income of the last 6 months ≤ 350 tax units (18,330,900); tapered above (Tax Code art. 206 num. 4). Excluded from the monthly withholding base under both procedures
3357	Flat 20% under art. 408 on the gross amount. The art. 206 num. 4 exemption is an exemption of the resident cédula-general regime and does not attach to a non-resident's flat withholding
3358	Dismissal indemnity
3359	Fully taxable employment income, withheld outside the ordinary monthly base: 20% where income exceeds 204 tax units (Tax Code art. 401-3); otherwise the table percentage for the average monthly income, i.e. the sum of the taxable payments of the previous 12 months divided by 12 (Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.8). Only the statutory-minimum indemnities of art. 206 nums. 1–3 are exempt, and only up to the legal minimum amount (art. 206 par. 1)
3360	Flat 20% under art. 408
3361	Equity / share incentives (stock options, restricted shares)
3362	Taxable employment income at exercise or vesting, on the spread between market value and exercise price; enters the ordinary monthly base and the art. 383 table. Subsequent disposal is a separate capital transaction, outside payroll
3363	Flat 20% under art. 408 where the underlying service was performed in Colombia. Where the service was performed abroad, foreign-source and not taxable
3364	Labour-service / contractor pay (non-employment work income)
3365	Art. 383 table applies, using "pagos mensualizados" — total contract value less mandatory health and pension contributions, divided by the months of the contract (art. 383 par. 2, added by Law 2277 of 2022 art. 8)
3366	Flat 20% under art. 408 for services rendered in Colombia. Technical services, technical assistance and consultancy rendered from abroad are also subject to withholding under arts. 24 and 408
3367	Benefits in kind (salary in kind, employer-paid housing, cars, insurance)
3368	Valued contractually or by expert appraisal; fully taxable as employment income within the ordinary monthly base. No notional valuation scale exists
3369	Flat 20% under art. 408 on the valued amount
3370	Third-party meal payments
3371	Not income up to 41 tax units per month (2,147,334) where salary ≤ 310 tax units (16,235,940); the excess is taxable (art. 387-1)
3372	The art. 387-1 exclusion is drafted as a non-income rule for the worker and applies on its own terms; any excess is withheld at the flat 20%
3373	Pension income paid by the employer
3374	Taxable only above 1,000 tax units per month (52,374,000) — art. 206 num. 5
3375	Flat 20%
3376	
3377	The residence switch is prospective, never retroactive. DIAN Concepto 011108 of 2026 rejected the argument that a foreign employee on a contract exceeding 183 days may be withheld under art. 383 from day one in anticipation of future residence. Withholding is determined at the moment of each payment, on the status then held. The engine must therefore withhold at 20% until residence is acquired, and switch to the art. 383 table from the payment following the date the 183-day test is met. There is no true-up of the earlier months through payroll; the employee reconciles in their own annual return. The 20% rate is lawful only while the employee is in fact a non-resident. A client instruction, even in writing, does not shift the liability: where the withholding is not made, the withholding agent answers for the amount it was required to withhold, with a right of reimbursement against the taxpayer, and any penalty is the agent's alone (Tax Code art. 370).
3378	Non-residents are IN SCOPE for Mercans Colombia payroll. A foreign national employed under a Colombian contract before acquiring residence is a real and common case (expatriate assignments, regional roles). The engine must carry a residence flag with an effective date and must support the mid-year switch.
3379	6.1 System Overview and Methodology
3380	Describe the country's income tax withholding system.
3381	Colombia operates a monthly, employer-administered advance-collection system called retención en la fuente sobre rentas de trabajo — withholding at source on work income. Its defining characteristics:
3382	Characteristic
3383	Colombia
3384	Nature
3385	Advance instalment of the employee's own annual income tax, not a final tax
3386	Frequency
3387	Monthly — one aggregated return per employer per month
3388	Cumulative?
3389	No. Non-cumulative. Each month stands alone under Procedure 1; Procedure 2 uses a fixed percentage recomputed twice a year
3390	Employer-borne income tax?
3391	None. Colombia has no employer payroll tax on income. The employer's cost is the social-security and levy burden of Module 5 only
3392	Basis
3393	A depurated monthly figure, neither gross pay nor the social-security contribution base
3394	Rate structure
3395	Progressive, expressed in tax units — seven bands, 0% to 39%
3396	Employee election
3397	The employee may request a higher rate in writing; there is no election of a lower rate
3398	Employer election
3399	The employer elects Procedure 1 or Procedure 2 per employee; once elected for a semester, Procedure 2 binds for six months
3400	Reconciliation
3401	The employee's own annual income-tax return, if required to file. The employer performs no year-end true-up
3402	Return
3403	Form 350, monthly, aggregating all withholdings of every type made by the employer
3404	Certificate
3405	Form 220, annual income and withholding certificate to each employee
3406	
3407	The employer is an agente de retención — a withholding agent. Its statutory obligations are to withhold the correct amount, to declare it on Form 350 by the deadline, to pay it, to issue the annual certificate, and to report the detail in the third-party information return.
3408	What types of taxes are withheld by the employer?
3409	For a standard employment relationship the employer withholds one tax only.
3410	Withheld item
3411	Applies to payroll?
3412	Basis
3413	Income tax withholding on work income (retención en la fuente sobre rentas de trabajo)
3414	Yes — the only payroll withholding
3415	Tax Code arts. 383, 385, 386, 388 (resident); arts. 408–415 (non-resident)
3416	Value-added tax withholding
3417	No — supplier payments, not payroll
3418	Tax Code art. 437-2
3419	Industry and commerce tax withholding
3420	No — municipal, on business turnover, not on salary
3421	Municipal statutes
3422	Stamp duty
3423	No
3424	Tax Code art. 519
3425	Financial-transactions tax
3426	No — bank-level, on movements of funds
3427	Tax Code art. 870
3428	Solidarity contribution to higher education
3429	Deducted, not a tax — a Labour Code art. 150 permitted deduction requiring no individual order, added by Law 1911 of 2018 art. 22
3430	Labour Code art. 150
3431	
3432	There is no local, municipal, departmental, church, solidarity or social surcharge on employment income in Colombia. The solidarity pension fund contribution (Module 5) is a social-security contribution, not a tax, and is itself non-taxable income to the employee.
3433	At what level are tax rates published (central or state)?
3434	Wholly central. Income tax and its withholding are national taxes, legislated by Congress in the Tax Code and administered by DIAN. There is:
3435	no departmental or municipal income tax on employment income;
3436	no sub-national rate, band, allowance or surcharge;
3437	no local variation of the tax unit.
3438	The two administrative acts that set the annual parameters are both national:
3439	Parameter
3440	Instrument
3441	2026
3442	Tax unit (UVT)
3443	Resolution DIAN issued each December under Tax Code art. 868
3444	Resolution DIAN 000238 of 15 December 2025 — COP 52,374
3445	Consolidated tax, customs and exchange rules
3446	The Single Tax Resolution
3447	Resolution DIAN 000227 of 23 September 2025
3448	
3449	The tax unit is uprated annually by the consumer price index for middle-income households over the year to 1 October. For 2026 that variation was certified at 5.17%, taking the unit from COP 49,799 to COP 52,374.
3450	Legal basis: Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 365, 368, 383, 385, 386, 388, 408–415, 437-2, 519, 868, 870; Resolution DIAN 000238 of 15 December 2025 art. 1; Resolution DIAN 000227 of 2025; Substantive Labour Code art. 150 (as amended by Law 1911 of 2018 art. 22); DIAN Concepto 011108 of 29 June 2026; DIAN Oficio 28380 of 2019.
3451	6.1.1 Taxable Income Definition
3452	Provide the statutory definition of 'Taxable Income'.
3453	The taxable base for withholding is the depurated payment of Tax Code article 388, added by Law 1819 of 2016 art. 18:
3454	"For the purposes of withholding at source, the payer may subtract from the payments or credits to account the following factors: 1. Income that the law exhaustively provides as not constituting taxable income or occasional gain. 2. The deductions referred to in article 387 of the Tax Code and the income that the law exhaustively provides as exempt. In any event, the total sum of deductions and exempt income may not exceed forty percent (40%) of the result of subtracting, from the amount of the payment or credit to account, the attributable income that does not constitute taxable income or occasional gain. This limitation shall not apply in the case of the payment of pensions… pension substitution indemnities and refunds of pension savings."
3455	An absolute annual cap is added by regulation — Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.6 par. 3, as modified by Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 9: deductions and exempt income may not exceed the particular limit of each benefit, nor 40%, nor the absolute value of 1,340 tax units per year (COP 70,181,160 for 2026).
3456	The scope of art. 383 defines what enters the base in the first place: "the taxable payments made … arising from an employment relationship, or a statutory and regulatory relationship, and payments received in respect of retirement, disability, old-age, survivors' and occupational-risk pensions."
3457	Explain how this base is derived from total gross earnings.
3458	(A)  TOTAL TAXABLE PAYMENTS OF THE MONTH     basic salary + overtime + night and rest-day premiums + commissions     + habitual bonuses + salary in kind + transport allowance     + service bonus [Procedure 2 only; Procedure 1 reads it separately]     EXCLUDING: severance savings and the interest on them (both procedures)(B)  LESS NON-TAXABLE INCOME  (ingresos no constitutivos de renta ni ganancia ocasional)     - employee mandatory PENSION contribution 4%                    art. 55     - employee SOLIDARITY PENSION FUND contribution 1% + 0.2-1.0%   art. 55     - employee mandatory HEALTH contribution 4%                     art. 56     - voluntary contributions to the individual pension account,       <= 25% of annual labour income, cap 2,500 tax units           art. 55     - qualifying third-party meal payments                          art. 387-1(C)  = (A) - (B)          <-- the reference figure for the 40% cap(D)  LESS DEDUCTIONS  (art. 387)     - housing-loan interest and monetary correction   monthly cap 100 tax units = 5,237,400     - prepaid health / health insurance               monthly cap  16 tax units =   837,984     - dependants: 10% of the month's gross labour income,                                                       monthly cap  32 tax units = 1,675,968(E)  LESS EXEMPT INCOME  (rentas exentas)     - voluntary pension funds + long-term savings accounts:       <= 30% of labour income, annual cap 3,800 tax units = 199,021,200   arts. 126-1, 126-4     - THEN, AND ONLY THEN, the 25% exemption of art. 206 num. 10,       computed on [(C) - D - other exempt income], annual cap 790 tax units = 41,375,460(F)  APPLY THE COMBINED CAP     (D + E) <= 40% of (C)     AND     (D + E) <= 1,340 tax units annually = 70,181,160     OUTSIDE the cap: 72 tax units per dependant per year, max 4 dependants                      = 3,770,928 each   (art. 336 num. 3, second paragraph)(G)  TAXABLE BASE = (C) - (D + E as capped) - (72-unit dependant deduction)(H)  CONVERT TO TAX UNITS:  base_UVT = (G) / 52,374(I)  APPLY THE ART. 383 TABLE  ->  withholding in tax units  ->  x 52,374  ->  COP
3459	The single most important ordering rule. Tax Code art. 206 num. 10 requires the 25% exemption to be computed "once there are subtracted from the total value of the labour payments received by the employee the income that does not constitute taxable income, the deductions and the other exempt income items other than the one established in the present numeral". Computing 25% of gross rather than 25% of the post-deduction figure overstates the exemption and under-withholds. It is computed last.
3460	Which tax-unit vintage applies to the annual caps. DIAN Concepto 015007 (int. 973) of 13 September 2023 confirms that the current year's tax unit is used — COP 52,374 for 2026 — "a clarification that is applicable for the purposes of procedure two … and the calculation of the fixed withholding percentage that must be carried out in the month of June".
3461	Open item — monthly apportionment of annual caps. The 790-tax-unit and 1,340-tax-unit caps are stated annually, yet withholding is monthly. Market practice, which Mercans follows, is to apportion them at one twelfth per month — 65.8333 tax units (COP 3,447,955) and 111.6667 tax units (COP 5,848,430) respectively — and to test each month against the apportioned figure. No regulation prescribes the apportionment expressly. The two figures are therefore an operating convention, not a statutory limit. Tax Code art. 206 num. 10 limits the exemption annually to 790 tax units and Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.6 par. 3 states 1,340 tax units annually; if the DIAN accepts an annual or cumulative test, the exempt amount in a high-pay month could exceed the apportioned figure, so the apportionment must be kept as a configurable parameter. Carried in the QA report.
3462	Deliverable: Step-by-step decision guide to determine the correct tax schedule for an employee
3463	START | +-- Q1. Is the payment employment income (relación laboral, o legal y reglamentaria)? |        NO  -> not payroll scope. Non-employment work income uses art. 383 par. 2 |               "pagos mensualizados" for residents; art. 408 for non-residents |        YES -> continue | +-- Q2. Is the employee a COLOMBIAN TAX RESIDENT at the date of this payment? |        Test (art. 10): >183 days present in any 365-day period, OR diplomatic |        status, OR the nationality-plus-connection tests | |        NO  -> Q2a. Is the service performed IN Colombia? |               |      NO  -> FOREIGN-SOURCE INCOME. NOT TAXABLE. NO WITHHOLDING. |               |             (DIAN Oficio 28380 of 2019) |               |      YES -> SCHEDULE N: FLAT 20% ON GROSS, art. 408. |               |             No depuration, no deductions, no exemptions. |               |             (DIAN Concepto 011108 of 2026) |               |             Re-test residence EVERY month; switch prospectively |               |             on the payment following the 183rd day. |        YES -> continue | +-- Q3. Is the payment SEVERANCE SAVINGS or the INTEREST on them? |        YES -> SCHEDULE C: art. 206 num. 4 exemption and taper. |               EXCLUDED from the monthly base under BOTH procedures. |        NO  -> continue | +-- Q4. Which withholding procedure has the employer elected for this employee? | |     PROCEDURE 1 (art. 385) |     | |     +-- Q4a. Is the payment the STATUTORY MINIMUM SERVICE BONUS? |     |        YES -> SCHEDULE B: separate read of the art. 383 table on the |     |               bonus alone, depurated in its own right. |     |        NO  -> SCHEDULE A: art. 383 table on the month's depurated base. |     |               If the period is shorter than 30 days: divide by days, |     |               multiply by 30, read the table PERCENTAGE, then apply that |     |               percentage to the actual payments of the period. |     | |     PROCEDURE 2 (art. 386) |     | |     +-- SCHEDULE D: apply the FIXED PERCENTAGE set in the preceding June or |            December to the month's depurated base. The service bonus is |            INCLUDED in that base; no separate read. |            New hire with no calculation yet -> read the table on the month's |            own taxable payments until the first semi-annual calculation. | +-- Q5. Has the employee requested a HIGHER rate in writing (art. 383 par. 3)?          YES -> apply the higher requested rate, effective from the month AFTER                 the request. It overrides upward only, never downward.

3566	6.2 Tax Rates and Logic
3567	What are the latest tax rates by tax type, residential status, and payment frequency?
3568	Tax type
3569	Resident
3570	Non-resident
3571	Frequency sensitivity
3572	Withholding on employment income
3573	Progressive 0 / 19 / 28 / 33 / 35 / 37 / 39%, art. 383
3574	Flat 20% on gross, art. 408
3575	None — the table is monthly; a sub-30-day period is grossed to 30 days under Procedure 1
3576	Withholding on non-employment work income
3577	Progressive art. 383 table on "pagos mensualizados" (art. 383 par. 2)
3578	Flat 20%, art. 408
3579	Contract value ÷ months of contract
3580	Withholding on employer-paid pension income
3581	Progressive art. 383 table, taxable only above 1,000 tax units per month
3582	Flat 20%
3583	Monthly
3584	Employee-requested higher rate
3585	Any rate above the computed one, on written request
3586	Not applicable
3587	From the month after the request

3589	None
3590	None
3591	—
3592	
3593	Note on the tax-unit denominated bands. Because the bands are in tax units, they reprice automatically each year. The 2026 boundaries in pesos are set out in the table in Module 6.1.1.
3594	How are rates applied for different frequencies?
3595	The withholding table is a monthly table, and the rates do not change with frequency. What changes is how a non-monthly period is mapped onto it.
3596	Frequency
3597	Procedure 1
3598	Procedure 2
3599	Monthly
3600	Read the table directly on the month's depurated base
3601	Apply the fixed percentage to the month's depurated base
3602	Semi-monthly
3603	Common Colombian practice pays the first half as an untaxed advance and computes the full month's withholding on the second payment. The statutorily rigorous alternative — grossing each half-month to 30 days and applying the resulting percentage to the actual half-month payments — is available under art. 385 and produces a smoother net
3604	Apply the fixed percentage to each payment, or to the month's total at month end
3605	Weekly / ten-day / fourteen-day
3606	Art. 385, sub-30-day rule: divide the payments by the number of days, multiply by 30, read the table percentage, then apply that percentage to the actual payments of the period
3607	Apply the fixed percentage to each payment
3608	A period longer than a month (arrears covering several months)
3609	See Module 9.2
3610	See Module 9.2
3611	
3612	The art. 385 sub-30-day rule is worth restating because it is a percentage rule, not an amount rule. The table is read to obtain a rate on the grossed-up notional monthly figure, and that rate is then applied to the real, smaller payment. Reading the table for an amount on the grossed-up figure would grossly over-withhold.
3613	How does tax applicability and calculation logic differ by residential status?
3614	Dimension
3615	Resident
3616	Non-resident
3617	Scope of taxation
3618	Worldwide income and wealth
3619	Colombian-source income only
3620	Employment income from a Colombian employer, work in Colombia
3621	Art. 383 progressive
3622	Art. 408, flat 20% on gross
3623	Employment income, work performed entirely abroad
3624	Taxable — worldwide basis
3625	Not taxable; no withholding
3626	Depuration
3627	Full art. 388 depuration
3628	None
3629	Non-taxable income (mandatory contributions)
3630	Deducted
3631	Not deducted
3632	Art. 387 deductions (housing interest, prepaid health, dependants)
3633	Available
3634	Not available
3635	25% exempt-income deduction
3636	Available, capped at 790 tax units per year
3637	Not available
3638	Tax-free band
3639	First 95 tax units per month
3640	None — 20% applies from the first peso
3641	Annual return
3642	Required where the art. 592 thresholds are exceeded
3643	Generally not required where withholding at source has been fully applied (art. 592 num. 2)
3644	Procedure election
3645	Procedure 1 or 2
3646	Neither — a flat rate, not a procedure
3647	Treaty relief
3648	Applies
3649	Applies — Colombia's treaty network (Spain, Chile, Switzerland, Canada, Mexico, South Korea, Portugal, India, Czech Republic, United Kingdom, France, Italy, Japan, and the Andean Community Decision 578) can reduce or eliminate the 20% where the employment article assigns taxing rights to the residence state. Treaty relief is a client decision requiring a residence certificate; Mercans applies it only on written client instruction
3650	
3651	The residence test — Tax Code art. 10. Presence exceeding 183 calendar days, continuous or not, in any 365-day period; where the period straddles two years, residence attaches to the year in which the 183rd day falls. Plus the diplomatic and nationality-plus-connection tests set out in Module 2.2.
3652	Operational rule. Carry a residence flag with an effective date on the employee record; re-evaluate it every payroll period; switch the schedule prospectively from the payment following the date the test is met. Never apply the change retroactively through payroll.
3653	Provide the tax calculation logic covering eligibility, joining month, termination month, mid-month joining, mid-month terminations, and mid-month salary revisions.
3654	Eligibility. Every employee is within the withholding system from the first payment. There is no registration threshold, no minimum-earnings exclusion and no employee opt-out. An employee whose depurated base falls in the 0% band is still within the system — the withholding is simply nil, and the payment is still reported on Form 350, in the electronic payroll XML, in the annual certificate and in the information return.
3655	Joining month and mid-month joining.
3656	Procedure
3657	Rule
3658	Procedure 1
3659	Apply the art. 385 sub-30-day rule: (payments ÷ days) × 30 → read the table percentage → apply that percentage to the actual payments of the period
3660	Procedure 2
3661	A new hire has no 12-month history, so no fixed percentage exists. Art. 386 directs that until the first semi-annual calculation the employer reads the table on the month's own taxable payments — i.e. Procedure 1 behaviour. At the next June or December calculation, the average uses the actual months of service as the divisor, not 13
3662	
3663	Termination month and mid-month termination.
3664	Compute the withholding on the salary and other ordinary payments for the days worked, using the same
3665	sub-30-day rule under Procedure 1 or the fixed percentage under Procedure 2.
3666	Exclude severance savings and the interest on them from the monthly base under both procedures; they
3667	carry the art. 206 num. 4 exemption and taper, driven by the average monthly income of the last six months of employment.
3668	Under Procedure 1, the accrued statutory minimum service bonus paid on termination retains its
3669	separate table read.
3670	A dismissal indemnity and a retirement bonus are outside the ordinary monthly base and are withheld separately. Where the employee's income exceeds 204 tax units (COP 10,684,296), withhold 20% on the indemnity (Tax Code art. 401-3; Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.13). Up to 204 tax units, divide the sum of the taxable payments received in the 12 months before the termination date by 12 (or by the months of service if fewer than 12), read the percentage in the withholding table of the termination year and apply it to the indemnity or bonus (Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.8). The 204-tax-unit test is applied to the employee's monthly income, and the final settlement paid on termination is not counted among the payments of the 12 months before the termination date (operating readings: the provisions cited do not state the period of the 204-tax-unit test or whether the final settlement counts).
3671	There is no termination-year true-up and no final tax reconciliation by the employer. The annual
3672	certificate (Form 220) records what was paid and what was withheld; the employee reconciles.
3673	Mid-month salary revision.
3674	Procedure
3675	Rule
3676	Procedure 1
3677	The month's payments are simply summed, whatever the segments, and the table is read once on the combined depurated base. No splitting is required for tax — unlike for social security, where a salary-variation change event is reported
3678	Procedure 2
3679	The fixed percentage is unchanged until the next June or December recalculation. A large mid-year increase therefore produces under-withholding for the remainder of the semester. The employee's remedy is a written request for a higher rate under art. 383 par. 3; the employer may not unilaterally increase the fixed percentage mid-semester
3680	
3681	A change of procedure. The procedure is elected by the employer, per employee. Procedure 2, once elected, applies for the whole six-month period following the June or December calculation. Market practice is to change procedure only at a semester boundary.
3682	Do tax rates vary by gender or age?
3683	No. There is no gender-differentiated or age-differentiated rate, band, allowance, deduction or exemption anywhere in the Colombian income-tax withholding system. The art. 383 table, the art. 387 deductions, the art. 206 exemptions and the art. 336 caps are identical for every employee.
3684	Two adjacent points that are sometimes mistaken for age variation:
3685	Pension income is taxable only on the monthly amount exceeding 1,000 tax units (COP 52,374,000)
3686	— Tax Code art. 206 num. 5. That is a rule about the type of income, not about the recipient's age, and it applies only where the taxpayer meets the Law 100 pension-entitlement conditions.
3687	Dependants aged 18 to 25 in higher or technical education generate a deduction for the taxpayer
3688	(Tax Code art. 387 par. 2 num. 2). That is a characteristic of the dependant, not of the employee.
3689	What is the minimum tax rate applicable?
3690	Zero per cent — for residents. The first band of the art. 383 table is 0% up to 95 tax units per month, which for 2026 is COP 4,975,530 of depurated base. There is no minimum withholding, no floor amount and no minimum-tax rule in the withholding system.
3691	Two important qualifications:
3692	95 tax units of depurated base is a considerably higher gross salary. Working the arithmetic
3693	backwards for an employee with only the mandatory contributions and the 25% exemption: a depurated base of 4,975,530 corresponds to a pre-exemption figure of 4,975,530 ÷ 0.75 = 6,634,040, which grossed up for the 9% employee contributions (4% + 4% + 1%) is approximately COP 7,290,000 of monthly gross salary. In practice, therefore, an employee earning up to roughly 7.3 million pesos a month — about 4.2 minimum wages — suffers no withholding at all.
3694	For non-residents the minimum is 20%, from the first peso. There is no exempt band, no allowance and
3695	no minimum threshold under art. 408.
3696	Is there a maximum? The top marginal rate is 39% above 2,300 tax units per month (COP 120,460,200). Since the base is depurated and progressive, the effective rate never reaches 39%.
3697	Employee-elected uplift. Tax Code art. 383 par. 3 permits the employee to request a higher withholding rate in writing, effective from the month after the request. This is used by employees who expect a balance due on their annual return. The engine must support an employee-elected rate that overrides upward only.
3698	Legal basis: Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 10, 206 nums. 4 and 5, 383 (and pars. 1, 2, 3), 385, 386, 387, 388, 408–415, 592; Law 2010 of 2019 art. 42; Law 2277 of 2022 art. 8; DIAN Concepto 011108 of 29 June 2026; DIAN Oficio 28380 of 2019; Andean Community Decision 578 and Colombia's bilateral double-tax treaties.
3699	6.3 Adjustments and Special Cases
3700	For annualized tax systems, which salary components must be projected?
3701	Colombia is not an annualised or cumulative withholding system, so there is no projection of future income. Procedure 1 looks only at the current month. Procedure 2 looks only backwards, at the preceding 12 months.
3702	This is a genuine structural difference from cumulative jurisdictions and must be configured deliberately: there is no annual estimate, no notional annual salary, no projected bonus and no year-end catch-up.
3703	What Procedure 2 requires — a backward-looking accumulator, not a projection:
3704	In JUNE and in DECEMBER of each year:  numerator = the sum of ALL taxable payments made to the employee over the              preceding 12 months, EXCLUDING severance savings and the              interest on severance savings  divisor   = 13          (or the actual number of months of service, if < 12)  average   = numerator / divisor  fixed %   = the art. 383 table result for `average`, expressed as a              percentage of `average`   (art. 383 par. 1)  The fixed % is then applied to each of the FOLLOWING SIX MONTHS' depurated bases.
3705	The divisor is 13, not 12. Tax Code art. 386 says "dividir por 13". The 13 divisor prorates the 13th-month effect of the statutory service bonus — which, unlike under Procedure 1, is not given a separate table read under Procedure 2 and instead sits inside the ordinary base. Using 12 over-withholds by roughly 8%.
3706	Components that must therefore be accumulated for 12 rolling months:
3707	Component
3708	In the accumulator?
3709	Basic salary
3710	Yes
3711	Overtime, night and rest-day premiums
3712	Yes
3713	Commissions and habitual bonuses
3714	Yes
3715	Statutory service bonus
3716	Yes
3717	Salary in kind
3718	Yes
3719	Transport allowance
3720	Yes
3721	Vacation pay and cash-compensated vacation
3722	Yes
3723	Severance savings
3724	No — expressly excluded by art. 386
3725	Interest on severance savings
3726	No — expressly excluded
3727	Non-taxable income and exempt income
3728	Deducted in the depuration, per art. 387 par. 1, which requires the art. 387 deductions to be used both in computing the fixed percentage and in computing the monthly base it is applied to
3729	
3730	Are year-end tax adjustments required?
3731	No — not by the employer. Colombia imposes no employer year-end tax reconciliation. The employer does not compute an annual liability, does not compare it with withholdings, and does not settle a difference through payroll. There is no equivalent of a P60 reconciliation or a year-end true-up run.
3732	What the employer must do at year end is certify and report, not reconcile:
3733	Obligation
3734	Deadline
3735	Content
3736	Form 220 — annual income and withholding certificate to each employee
3737	Last business day of March
3738	Tax Code arts. 378–379: the employer's and employee's identification, the total payments by concept, the total withheld, and the employee's own declarations where the employee is a non-filer (art. 380). Form prescribed by Resolution DIAN 000022 of 20 February 2024
3739	Information return, Format 2276 version 4 — income and withholdings on work income
3740	May–June, by tax number
3741	Resolution DIAN 000227 of 2025 arts. 1.3.7.1.1 and 1.3.9.1
3742	December recalculation of the fixed percentage (Procedure 2 only)
3743	December
3744	Sets the rate for January–June of the following year
3745	Reparameterisation for the new tax unit
3746	1 January
3747	Every band boundary and cap reprices
3748	
3749	The employee reconciles, not the employer. An employee whose income exceeds the Tax Code art. 592 filing thresholds files an annual income-tax return, in which the withholdings are credited against the computed liability and any excess is refunded or any shortfall paid.
3750	Are there minimum or higher tax requirements if employees fail to submit a tax ID or social security number?
3751	No. Colombia has no penalty withholding rate for a missing tax identifier — no equivalent of an emergency code, a flat penalty rate or a default maximum rate.
3752	The reasons are structural:
3753	Employees are generally not required to be registered in the tax registry. The employer's withholding
3754	obligation arises from the payment, not from the employee's registration status. The employee's identity-document number is the identifier used throughout.
3755	The identity document is a precondition of employment itself, not of payroll. An employment contract
3756	requires an identified person; social-security affiliation requires an identity document; the electronic payroll XML will not validate without a document type and number from the closed code list.
3757	What actually happens where identifiers are missing or wrong:
3758	Missing item
3759	Consequence
3760	Identity document type or number
3761	The electronic payroll XML fails validation (rule ZB01 and the global rules). The document cannot be transmitted, so the employer loses the tax deductibility of that payroll cost. Contribution-return filing also fails
3762	Employer tax number or verification digit
3763	Validation rules NIE038 / NIE041 reject the document
3764	Institution codes (health insurer, pension administrator, occupational-risk insurer, fund)
3765	The contribution return fails or misdirects contributions
3766	Employee declarations for deductions (dependants, housing interest, prepaid health)
3767	No penalty — the employee simply loses the deduction and is over-withheld. The remedy is the employee's own annual return
3768	Dependant's identity document
3769	The deduction is not sustainable on audit, and the information return is rejected
3770	
3771	The one direction in which the rate can move upward is voluntary: the employee may request a higher rate in writing under art. 383 par. 3.
3772	What is the income tax and social security refund procedure?
3773	Colombia has two distinct refund routes, one for tax and one for contributions, and neither is a payroll operation in the ordinary case.
3774	(a) Over-withheld income tax.
3775	Route
3776	Who initiates
3777	Mechanism
3778	Within the same fiscal year, before the Form 350 is filed
3779	Employer
3780	The employer corrects the withholding in a subsequent period of the same year and adjusts the aggregated Form 350 figure. This is the normal, and preferred, route
3781	After the Form 350 has been filed
3782	Employer
3783	File a correction return for the affected period. Where the correction reduces the declared value, Tax Code art. 589 applies and the correction must be filed within one year of the original filing deadline
3784	Reimbursement of amounts withheld in excess or wrongly withheld (retenciones practicadas en exceso o indebidamente)
3785	Employee requests, employer executes
3786	Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.16: on the employee's written request with evidence, the employer reimburses the excess and deducts the reimbursed amount from the withholdings of the following period, carrying any surplus forward until absorbed. The employer must keep the employee's written request
3787	After the year has closed and the employee has filed
3788	Employee
3789	The excess is credited on the employee's own annual return and refunded by the tax authority under the general refund procedure (Tax Code arts. 850 et seq.)
3790	
3791	(b) Over-paid social-security contributions.
3792	Route
3793	Mechanism
3794	Correction planilla (type N)
3795	The employer files paired records — an A group with the originally settled values and a C group with the corrected values. Note the structural constraint: the sum of the contribution bases and of the day counts in the C records must be greater than or equal to those in the A records, so the correction planilla is designed for under-declaration, not over-declaration
3796	Refund claim to the administering entity
3797	For a genuine overpayment, the claim is made directly to the health insurer, pension administrator, occupational-risk insurer, family compensation fund, apprenticeship service or family welfare institute that received the money. Each has its own procedure and evidentiary requirements
3798	Reimbursement to the employee
3799	Where the employee's share was over-deducted, the employer reimburses the employee through payroll and pursues the recovery from the receiving entity itself. The employer carries the cash-flow and the process risk
3800	UGPP
3801	Where the overpayment arises from an audit adjustment, the UGPP process governs
3802	
3803	Timing. Contribution refunds are materially slower than tax corrections — commonly several months — because each receiving entity processes independently. Mercans practice is therefore to reimburse the employee promptly through payroll on the employer's authority and to pursue recovery separately.
3804	Deliverable: Detailed worked examples for a standard resident employee, a non-resident employee, and an irregular payment
3805	Example 1 — standard resident employee, Procedure 1, August 2026.
3806	Employee on COP 8,000,000, no deductions declared. Fully worked in Module 6.1.1 above: depurated base COP 5,460,000 = 104.2502 tax units → 19% band → withholding COP 92,049.30; effective rate on gross 1.15%.
3807	Example 2 — resident employee with declared deductions, Procedure 1, August 2026.
3808	Same employee, but now declaring: housing-loan interest COP 1,200,000 per month; prepaid health premium COP 700,000 per month; two dependants (children aged 8 and 12).
3809	Line
3810	Computation
3811	COP
3812	(A) Total taxable payments
3813	
3814	8,000,000.00
3815	(B) Non-taxable — pension 4% + solidarity 1% + health 4%
3816

3818	(C) Subtotal
3819

3823	(1,200,000.00)
3824	(D2) Prepaid health
3825	Declared 700,000; monthly cap 16 tax units = 837,984 → within cap
3826	(700,000.00)
3827	(D3) Dependants, art. 387
3828	10% × 8,000,000 = 800,000; monthly cap 32 tax units = 1,675,968 → within cap
3829	(800,000.00)
3830	Subtotal deductions (D)
3831	
3832	(2,700,000.00)
3833	(E) Exempt income — 25%
3834	25% × (7,280,000 − 2,700,000) = 25% × 4,580,000
3835	(1,145,000.00)
3836	— 790-unit apportioned monthly figure
3837	3,447,955 → within
3838	ok
3839	(D + E) before cap
3840	2,700,000 + 1,145,000
3841	3,845,000.00
3842	(F) 40% cap
3843	40% × 7,280,000 = 2,912,000 → the cap bites; (D + E) is limited to 2,912,000
3844	(2,912,000.00)
3845	— 1,340-unit apportioned monthly figure
3846	5,848,430 → not reached
3847	ok
3848	Subtotal after the capped deductions
3849	7,280,000 − 2,912,000
3850	4,368,000.00
3851	Outside the cap: additional dependants deduction, art. 336 num. 3
3852	72 tax units × 2 dependants ÷ 12 = 12 units per month = 12 × 52,374
3853	(628,488.00)
3854	(G) Taxable base
3855	4,368,000 − 628,488
3856	3,739,512.00
3857	(H) In tax units
3858	3,739,512 ÷ 52,374
3859	71.4002
3860	(I) Band
3861	≤ 95 tax units → 0%
3862	
3863	Withholding
3864	
3865	COP 0.00
3866	
3867	Note the two ordering effects this example demonstrates: the 25% exemption is computed on the post-deduction figure (4,580,000, not 7,280,000), and the 72-unit-per-dependant deduction is applied after and outside the 40% cap. Both children generate both the art. 387 deduction and the art. 336 deduction, per Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 3.
3868	Example 3 — non-resident employee, August 2026.
3869	Facts: Spanish national, hired on a Colombian indefinite-term contract on 1 July 2026, working in Bogotá, salary COP 15,000,000 per month. As at the August payroll the employee has been present approximately 45 days and is not yet a Colombian tax resident.
3870	Line
3871	Computation
3872	COP
3873	Gross payment
3874	
3875	15,000,000.00
3876	Article 388 depuration
3877	Not applicable to a non-resident
3878	—
3879	Non-taxable income (contributions)
3880	Not deducted
3881	—
3882	25% exempt income
3883	Not available
3884	—
3885	Tax-free band
3886	None
3887	—
3888	Withholding — art. 408, flat 20%
3889	20% × 15,000,000
3890	3,000,000.00
3891	Employee social security — health 4%
3892	Contribution base 15,000,000
3893	(600,000.00)
3894	Employee social security — pension 4%
3895	Unless pension affiliation is voluntary for the employee under Law 100 of 1993 art. 15 num. 2 (Law 797 of 2003 art. 3) and the per-employee option is not exercised
3896	(600,000.00)
3897	Solidarity fund 1%
3898	15,000,000 ≥ 4 minimum wages (7,003,620)
3899	(150,000.00)
3900	Net pay
3901	15,000,000 − 3,000,000 − 1,350,000
3902	10,650,000.00
3903	
3904	The switch. The employee exceeds 183 days of presence (the 184th day) on or about 30 December 2026. From the first payment after that date the engine switches to the art. 383 progressive table. Had the same employee been a resident in August, the withholding would have been COP 1,190,532 rather than COP 3,000,000 — depurated base COP 10,237,500 = 195.4691 tax units, in the 28% band. No retroactive adjustment is made through payroll; the employee reconciles in their own annual return. Where the Colombia–Spain double-tax treaty assigns the taxing right differently, relief is applied only on written client instruction supported by a residence certificate.
3905	Example 4 — irregular payment: a retention bonus paid to a resident, Procedure 1, September 2026.
3906	Employee on COP 8,000,000 receiving a one-off retention bonus of COP 20,000,000. The bonus is contractual, is not the statutory service bonus, and is subject to a valid written non-salary pact.
3907	Line
3908	Computation
3909	COP
3910	Salary
3911

3916	Total remuneration for the month
3917

3923	40% × 28,000,000
3924	11,200,000.00
3925	Excess forced back into the contribution base
3926	20,000,000 − 11,200,000

3933	1,512,000.00
3934	Tax: (A) total taxable payments
3935	The non-salary pact has no tax effect — the bonus is fully taxable

3950	40% × 26,488,000 = 10,595,200 → 3,447,955 is within
3951	ok
3952	(G) Taxable base

3959	> 360 and ≤ 640 → marginal 33%
3960

3963

3966	4,994,989.65
3967	
3968	Two lessons this example makes concrete: the 40% clamp forced COP 8,800,000 of a "non-salary" bonus back into the contribution base, and the apportioned monthly 790-tax-unit figure on the 25% exemption bit for the first time, limiting the exemption to COP 3,447,955 rather than the arithmetic 25% of COP 6,622,000.

3971	Module 7: Deductions, Exemptions & Benefits
3972	Purpose: To define rules for all items that adjust income or pay.
3973	7.1 Statutory Tax Reliefs, Deductions, and Exemptions
3974	Deliverable: For EACH statutory tax relief, deduction, exemption, or other tax advantage, provide Name and Definition, Eligibility Rules, Statutory Limits & Amounts, Calculation Method, Application Point, Employee Declaration Requirements
3975	Colombia's reliefs fall into three legally distinct classes, and the class determines where each item sits in the depuration and which caps it consumes. Getting the class wrong is the most common source of withholding error.
3976	Class
3977	Legal name
3978	Where deducted
3979	Consumes the 40% / 1,340-unit cap?
3980	A
3981	Non-taxable income (ingresos no constitutivos de renta ni ganancia ocasional)
3982	Step (B), before the cap reference figure is struck
3983	No
3984	B
3985	Deductions (deducciones) and exempt income (rentas exentas)
3986	Steps (D) and (E)
3987	Yes
3988	C
3989	Outside the cap
3990	After the cap
3991	No
3992	
3993	A1 — Mandatory pension contribution
3994	Element
3995	Detail
3996	Definition
3997	The employee's compulsory 4% pension contribution on the social-security contribution base
3998	Eligibility
3999	Every employee who contributes to pension. Not available to an employed old-age pensioner, who makes no pension contribution
4000	Limit
4001	4% of the contribution base, itself capped at 25 minimum wages = COP 43,772,625 → maximum COP 1,750,905 per month
4002	Calculation
4003	4% × contribution base, rounded up to the nearest 100
4004	Application point
4005	Step (B), non-taxable income
4006	Declaration
4007	None — computed by the engine
4008	Source
4009	Tax Code art. 55
4010	
4011	A2 — Solidarity pension fund contribution
4012	Element
4013	Detail
4014	Definition
4015	The employee-only surcharge funding the solidarity and subsistence sub-accounts
4016	Eligibility
4017	Contribution base ≥ 4 minimum wages (COP 7,003,620): 1% split 0.5% solidarity / 0.5% subsistence; ≥ 16 minimum wages adds the banded 0.2%–1.0%, all to the subsistence account
4018	Limit
4019	1.0% to 2.0% of the contribution base
4020	Calculation
4021	Banded — see Module 5.1.1
4022	Application point
4023	Step (B), non-taxable income. Confirmed by field 24 of the information return, which groups the pension and solidarity contributions on a single line
4024	Declaration
4025	None
4026	Source
4027	Tax Code art. 55; Law 100 of 1993 arts. 20 and 27 (as amended by Law 797 of 2003 arts. 7–8)
4028	
4029	A3 — Mandatory health contribution
4030	Element
4031	Detail
4032	Definition
4033	The employee's compulsory 4% health contribution
4034	Eligibility
4035	Every employee
4036	Limit
4037	4% of the contribution base, maximum COP 1,750,905 per month
4038	Calculation
4039	4% × contribution base, rounded up to the nearest 100
4040	Application point
4041	Step (B)
4042	Declaration
4043	None
4044	Source
4045	Tax Code art. 56
4046	
4047	A4 — Voluntary contributions to the individual pension account
4048	Element
4049	Detail
4050	Definition
4051	Voluntary contributions made by the employee, or by the employer on the employee's behalf, to the employee's individual account in the private pension regime
4052	Eligibility
4053	Employees affiliated to the private individual-account regime
4054	Limit
4055	The lower of 25% of annual labour income and 2,500 tax units = COP 130,935,000 per year
4056	Calculation
4057	Deducted at the amount contributed, within the cap
4058	Application point
4059	Step (B) — more valuable than exempt income, because it is deducted before the 40% cap reference figure is struck
4060	Declaration
4061	Amount and period, evidenced by a pension administrator certificate
4062	Clawback
4063	A withdrawal that does not meet the qualifying conditions is withheld at 35%
4064	Source
4065	Tax Code art. 55
4066	
4067	A5 — Third-party meal payments
4068	Element
4069	Detail
4070	Definition
4071	Payments made by the employer to a third party for the food of the employee or the employee's family, including the purchase of meal vouchers or tickets. "Familia" means the spouse or permanent partner, the children and the parents of the employee
4072	Eligibility
4073	Two independent conditions, both must hold: (i) the employee's salary does not exceed 310 tax units = COP 16,235,940; and (ii) the payment is made to a third party — cash paid to the employee is salary and gets none of this treatment
4074	Limit
4075	Where the payments in the month exceed 41 tax units = COP 2,147,334, the excess is taxable employment income subject to withholding
4076	Calculation
4077	Up to 41 tax units: not income to the employee, income to the third party supplying the food. Above 41 tax units: the excess enters the taxable base
4078	Application point
4079	Step (B) as a non-income exclusion
4080	Declaration
4081	Employer records; the information return reports the within-limit amount (field 32) and the excess (field 8) separately
4082	Excluded
4083	Representation expenses are expressly outside this rule and are deductible for the employer
4084	Source
4085	Tax Code art. 387-1
4086	
4087	B1 — Housing-loan interest and monetary correction
4088	Element
4089	Detail
4090	Definition
4091	Interest and monetary correction paid on a loan for the acquisition of the employee's dwelling, or on a housing leasing contract
4092	Eligibility
4093	Employees paying qualifying housing-loan interest
4094	Limit
4095	Monthly cap 100 tax units = COP 5,237,400 (the monthly figure is derived from the annual 1,200-unit limit of Tax Code art. 119; the monthly apportionment is market practice — see the QA report)
4096	Calculation
4097	The lower of the amount paid and the cap
4098	Application point
4099	Step (D), inside the 40% and 1,340-unit caps
4100	Declaration
4101	Amount paid, evidenced by a lender certificate
4102	Source
4103	Tax Code arts. 119 and 387 first paragraph

4106	Element
4107	Detail
4108	Definition
4109	Payments for prepaid medicine or health insurance covering the employee, the spouse, the children and the dependants
4110	Eligibility
4111	The provider must be supervised by the National Health Superintendence (prepaid medicine) or the Financial Superintendence (health insurance)
4112	Limit
4113	16 tax units per month = COP 837,984
4114	Calculation

4117	Step (D)

4123	B3 — Dependants deduction
4124	Element
4125	Detail
4126	Definition
4127	A deduction of 10% of the month's gross labour income where the employee has one or more qualifying dependants
4128	Eligibility
4129	At least one dependant as defined in Tax Code art. 387 par. 2
4130	Limit
4131	32 tax units per month = COP 1,675,968. Note: the deduction is 10% of gross regardless of the number of dependants — it does not multiply
4132	Calculation
4133	min(10% × gross labour income of the month, 1,675,968)
4134	Application point
4135	Step (D)
4136	Declaration
4137	The dependants and the basis of dependency
4138	Source
4139	Tax Code art. 387
4140	
4141	Who is a dependant — Tax Code art. 387 par. 2, numerals 2 and 3 as amended by Law 2411 of 2024 art. 2:
4142	#
4143	Dependant
4144	1
4145	Children up to 18
4146	2

4148	3

4150	4

4152	5

4156	Element
4157	Detail
4158	Definition
4159	Contributions to voluntary pension funds (art. 126-1) and to construction-savings or long-term-savings accounts (art. 126-4)
4160	Eligibility
4161	Any employee; employer contributions on the employee's behalf count toward the same cap
4162	Limit
4163	The lower of 30% of labour income and 3,800 tax units per year = COP 199,021,200, shared between the two vehicles
4164	Calculation
4165	Amount contributed within the shared cap
4166	Application point
4167	Step (E), exempt income, inside the 40% and 1,340-unit caps
4168	Declaration
4169	Amount and period, evidenced by fund certificates
4170	Employer deduction
4171	Employer contributions are deductible for the employer up to 3,800 tax units per employee (art. 126-1 first paragraph)
4172	Source
4173	Tax Code arts. 126-1 and 126-4
4174	
4175	B5 — The 25% exempt-income deduction
4176	Element
4177	Detail
4178	Definition
4179	The general labour-income exemption of twenty-five per cent of total labour payments — "Twenty-five percent (25%) of the total value of the labour payments"
4180	Eligibility
4181	Every resident employee. Not available to a non-resident withheld under art. 408
4182	Limit
4183	790 tax units per year = COP 41,375,460; apportioned monthly at 65.8333 units = COP 3,447,955
4184	Calculation
4185	CRITICAL ORDERING — it is computed after every other item has been deducted: "The calculation of this exempt income shall be made once there are subtracted from the total value of the labour payments received by the employee the income that does not constitute taxable income, the deductions and the other exempt income items other than the one established in the present numeral." It is computed LAST, on the figure net of A, B1–B4
4186	Application point
4187	Step (E), last item, then subject to the caps
4188	Declaration
4189	None — automatic
4190	Excluded
4191	Does not apply to severance savings, to income already excluded by other rules, or to the taxable part of a pension (art. 206 par. 2)
4192	Source
4193	Tax Code art. 206 num. 10, as amended by Law 2277 of 2022 art. 2
4194	
4195	B6 — Severance savings exemption
4196	Element
4197	Detail
4198	Definition
4199	Exemption of statutory severance savings and the interest on them
4200	Eligibility
4201	Exempt in full where the employee's average monthly income over the last 6 months of employment does not exceed 350 tax units = COP 18,330,900
4202	Limit
4203	Above that, the untaxed portion tapers:
4204	
4205	Average monthly salary
4206	Untaxed portion
4207	350 – 410 tax units
4208	90%
4209	410 – 470 tax units
4210	80%
4211	470 – 530 tax units
4212	60%
4213	530 – 590 tax units
4214	40%
4215	590 – 650 tax units
4216	20%
4217	From 650 tax units
4218	0%
4219	
4220	Element
4221	Detail
4222	Application point
4223	Severance savings and their interest are excluded from the monthly withholding base entirely, under both procedures (Tax Code arts. 385 and 386). The exemption and taper are applied when the payment is made
4224	Declaration
4225	None; the engine must maintain the rolling 6-month average
4226	Source
4227	Tax Code art. 206 num. 4
4228	
4229	B7 — Pension income exemption
4230	Element
4231	Detail
4232	Definition
4233	Retirement, invalidity, old-age, survivor and occupational-risk pensions
4234	Limit
4235	Taxable only on the monthly amount exceeding 1,000 tax units = COP 52,374,000
4236	Eligibility
4237	The taxpayer must meet the Law 100 pension-entitlement conditions; the treatment extends to foreign and multilateral-organisation pensions
4238	Source
4239	Tax Code art. 206 num. 5 and par. 3
4240	
4241	B8 — Statutory indemnity exemptions
4242	Element
4243	Detail
4244	Definition
4245	Accident and illness indemnities (num. 1), maternity-protection indemnities (num. 2), and the burial expenses of the employee (num. 3)
4246	Limit
4247	Art. 206 par. 1 limits numerals 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 to the statutory minimum amounts: "operates only on the amounts corresponding to the statutory minimum … the excess is not exempt"
4248	Source
4249	Tax Code art. 206 nums. 1–3 and par. 1
4250	
4251	C1 — The additional dependants deduction
4252	Element
4253	Detail
4254	Definition
4255	A deduction of 72 tax units per dependant per year, maximum 4 dependants
4256	Eligibility
4257	Dependants as defined in art. 387 par. 2
4258	Limit
4259	72 tax units = COP 3,770,928 per dependant per year; maximum 4 dependants = COP 15,083,712 per year; monthly 6 tax units = COP 314,244 per dependant
4260	Calculation
4261	Deducted after and outside the 40% and 1,340-unit caps — "in addition to the limit established in the preceding paragraph"
4262	Application point
4263	Step (G), outside the caps
4264	Declaration
4265	Number of dependants and each dependant's identity document, relationship and basis of dependency. The information return (field 34) requires the document, not merely a count
4266	Stacking
4267	May be taken together with B3 for the same dependant. Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 3: "the same dependant shall give rise to only one of these two deductions, except where the taxpayer has income from an employment or statutory and regulatory relationship, in which case both deductions may be applied for the same dependant"
4268	Source
4269	Tax Code art. 336 num. 3, second paragraph; Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 3
4270	
4271	Not available against withholding. The 1% / 240-tax-unit invoice-based deduction of Tax Code art. 336 num. 5 is expressly excluded — "shall not be taken into account for the calculation of withholding at source" — confirmed by DIAN Concepto 015007 (int. 973) of 13 September 2023: "this deduction is confined to the determination of the net income of the general basket and does not apply for the purposes of withholding at source." It is available only in the employee's own annual return.
4272	Outside the caps by statute, but outside Mercans scope. Tax Code art. 206 par. 4 and Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.6 par. 3 place numerals 6 to 9 of art. 206, the prima especial and the prima de costo de vida outside the 40% and 1,340-unit limits. These attach to military, judicial, diplomatic and public university staff — out of Mercans private-sector scope — but the engine must carry the flag so that it does not silently clamp them if such an employee is loaded.
4273	Are salary sacrifice arrangements allowed?
4274	There is no salary-sacrifice regime in Colombian law, and the nearest Colombian equivalents work in a materially different way.
4275	Arrangement
4276	Available?
4277	Effect
4278	Classic salary sacrifice — the employee gives up contractual salary in exchange for a non-cash benefit, reducing both the tax and the contribution base
4279	No. There is no statutory mechanism, no tax-authority-approved structure and no doctrine supporting it. Labour Code art. 13 makes the minimum labour rights irrenounceable, and art. 143 presumes any differential remuneration unjustified
4280	—
4281	Non-salary pact (Labour Code art. 128) — the parties agree in writing that a benefit or allowance is not salary
4282	Yes, and this is the Colombian workhorse. But it is not a sacrifice: the employee gives up nothing, and the payment must genuinely be a benefit or allowance, not consideration for the service
4283	Removes the payment from the statutory-benefit base, the overtime base and the contribution base — but only up to the 40% clamp of Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30. No tax effect whatsoever: the payment remains fully taxable employment income
4284	Employer contributions to voluntary pension funds
4285	Yes
4286	Exempt income to the employee within the shared 30% / 3,800-unit cap; deductible for the employer up to 3,800 units per employee
4287	Employer contributions to the individual pension account
4288	Yes
4289	Non-taxable income to the employee within the 25% / 2,500-unit cap
4290	Third-party meal payments and vouchers
4291	Yes
4292	Not income to the employee within the art. 387-1 thresholds
4293	Salary in kind (Labour Code art. 129)
4294	Yes, but it is the opposite of a sacrifice — it is salary, fully contributory and fully taxable, capped at 50% of total salary and 30% at the minimum wage
4295	
4296	All-inclusive salary (Labour Code art. 132 num. 2)
4297	Yes
4298	Reduces the contribution and payroll-levy bases to 70% — the closest Colombia comes to a structural contribution reduction, available only above 13 minimum wages
4299	
4300	The key configuration point. Colombian benefit structuring is a social-security optimisation, never a tax optimisation. A non-salary pact reduces contributions (within 40%) and statutory benefits; it does not reduce taxable income by one peso. Any engine that treats a non-salary flag as a tax exclusion will systematically under-withhold.
4301	List common types of post-tax deductions and any specific ordering rules or limitations.

4379	The attachability limits — Labour Code arts. 154–156:
4380	Rule
4381	Effect
4382	Art. 154
4383	Salary is not attachable as a general rule
4384	Art. 155
4385	The excess over the minimum wage is attachable up to one fifth (20%) of that excess
4386	Art. 156
4387	Exception: for maintenance obligations and debts owed to cooperatives, up to 50% of the whole salary, including the minimum-wage portion, is attachable
4388	
4389	The overriding limitation — Labour Code art. 149 num. 2. No deduction or set-off may reduce pay below the minimum wage or into the non-attachable portion without a court order, even where the employee has authorised it in writing. This governs every voluntary deduction in the table, and is the reason priorities 5 to 8 carry "No" in the last column.
4390	Payroll lending specifically. Law 1527 of 2012 governs libranza deductions. The employer must deduct and remit the instalment to the lender once the authorisation is in place; the aggregate of libranza deductions must leave the employee with at least 50% of net salary after all statutory deductions. Labour Code art. 149 num. 3 makes the employer liable in damages for failing to make an authorised, lawful deduction.
