4433	General principle
4434	
4435	Not exempt, contrary to a common misconception: the transport allowance, the connectivity allowance, the statutory service bonus, vacation pay, overtime and premiums, dismissal indemnities beyond the statutory-minimum indemnities of art. 206 nums. 1–3, and any bonus however labelled. A non-salary pact under Labour Code art. 128 confers no tax exemption of any kind.
4436	Legal basis: Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 55, 56, 119, 126-1, 126-4, 206 (nums. 1–5, 10 and pars. 1, 2, 4), 336 (nums. 3 and 5), 383, 385, 386, 387, 387-1, 388; Law 2277 of 2022 art. 2; Law 2411 of 2024 art. 2; Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.6 par. 3 (as modified by Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 9); Decree 2231 of 2023 art. 3; DIAN Concepto 015007 int. 973 of 13 September 2023; Substantive Labour Code arts. 13, 128, 129, 132, 143, 149, 150, 151, 154, 155, 156, 230 (art. 150 as amended by Law 1911 of 2018 art. 22); Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30; Law 1527 of 2012.
4437	7.2 Benefits in Kind (BIK)
4438	Are non-cash benefits taxed via the standard payroll system, or a separate employer-paid regime?
4439	Through the standard payroll system. Colombia has no fringe-benefits tax, no separate employer-paid benefits regime and no parallel filing for non-cash benefits.
4440	A benefit in kind is taxed by being classified. If it is salary in kind under Labour Code art. 129, it is ordinary taxable employment income and ordinary contributory salary, and it flows through the normal gross-to-net calculation. If it falls within Labour Code art. 128 or a specific tax exclusion, it is neither.
4441	Question
4442	Colombia
4443	Separate fringe-benefits tax?
4444	No
4445	Separate employer-paid regime?
4446	No
4447	Separate return?
4448	No — reported in the ordinary electronic payroll XML and information return
4449	Notional or administrative valuation scales (company car tables, accommodation scales)?
4450	None exist
4451	Employer-borne or employee-borne?
4452	Employee-borne — the employee is taxed on the value, and the employer withholds
4453	
4454	What is the definition of fringe benefits and how is the taxable amount evaluated?
4455	Labour Code art. 129 (Law 50 of 1990 art. 16), verbatim:
4456	"1. Salary in kind consists of any part of the ordinary and permanent remuneration that the employee receives as direct consideration for the service, such as food, accommodation or clothing that the employer supplies to the employee or to their family, save for the stipulation provided in article [128] of this law. 2. Salary in kind must be expressly valued in every employment contract. In the absence of a stipulation or of agreement on its real value it shall be assessed by expert appraisal, without it being able to constitute more than fifty percent (50%) of the total salary. 3. However, where an employee earns the legal minimum wage, the value attributed to salary in kind may not exceed thirty percent (30%)."
4457	Valuation method. Three rules, in order:
4458	Contractual valuation. The value must be expressly stated in the employment contract. This is
4459	the primary rule and the one the engine relies on.
4460	Expert appraisal (estimación pericial) where the parties have not agreed a value or disagree on
4461	the real value.
4462	Statutory ceilings. Salary in kind may not exceed 50% of total salary, or 30% where the
4463	employee earns the legal minimum wage.
4464	There is no published valuation schedule for benefits in kind in Colombia. No company-car table, no accommodation scale, no notional-value list. This was searched for and none exists — and the absence is principled rather than accidental: art. 129 num. 2 routes an unvalued benefit to expert appraisal, not to a published scale. The engine therefore requires a contractual value on every in-kind wage type.
4465	If a separate FBT regime exists, describe it in detail.
4466	Not Applicable — no fringe-benefits tax regime exists in Colombia. Non-cash benefits are taxed to the employee through ordinary payroll withholding, or are excluded from income by a specific statutory rule. There is no employer-level benefits tax, no separate rate, no separate base and no separate return.
4467	What is the calculation logic for fringe benefit tax?
4468	Not Applicable — there is no fringe benefit tax. The calculation logic for a benefit in kind is therefore the ordinary classification decision tree:
4469	For each benefit provided:Q1. Is it ordinary and permanent consideration for the service?    YES -> SALARY IN KIND (Labour Code art. 129)           * value it in the contract, or by expert appraisal           * cap: <= 50% of total salary;  <= 30% at the legal minimum wage           * enters the CONTRIBUTION base, the PAYROLL-LEVY base,             the STATUTORY-BENEFIT base           * FULLY TAXABLE within the ordinary monthly base    NO  -> Q2Q2. Is it a habitual or occasional benefit that the parties have EXPRESSLY    agreed IN WRITING is not salary?  (art. 128 - food, housing, clothing,    extra-statutory bonuses)    YES -> NOT SALARY for labour-law purposes           * out of the statutory-benefit base and the overtime base permanently           * out of the contribution base ONLY UP TO the 40% clamp;             the excess goes back in (Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30)           * STILL FULLY TAXABLE - a non-salary pact has NO tax effect    NO  -> Q3Q3. Is it given not to enrich the employee but to let them do the job -    representation expenses, means of transport, work tools, protective    equipment, statutory work clothing (art. 230)?    YES -> NOT SALARY and NOT TAXABLE. Outside every base.    NO  -> Q4Q4. Is it a MEAL benefit provided through a THIRD PARTY (restaurant,    voucher, meal card)?    YES -> Tax Code art. 387-1 regime:           * salary <= 310 tax units (16,235,940)  AND           * monthly amount <= 41 tax units (2,147,334)           -> not income to the employee; income to the third party           * excess over 41 tax units -> taxable employment income           * CASH paid to the employee for food is SALARY - none of this applies    NO  -> Q5Q5. Is it employer-paid PREPAID HEALTH or HEALTH INSURANCE?    YES -> not a statutory exclusion. It is a taxable benefit, but the           EMPLOYEE may deduct up to 16 tax units per month (837,984)           against the withholding base (art. 387 lits. a and b)    NO  -> Q6Q6. Is it an employer contribution to a VOLUNTARY PENSION FUND?    YES -> exempt income to the employee within the shared 30% /           3,800-tax-unit annual cap; deductible for the employer up to           3,800 tax units per employee (art. 126-1)    NO  -> default: taxable employment income, and salary unless a valid           written non-salary pact applies
4470	Are benefits-in-kind also subject to withholding tax?
4471	Yes — every taxable benefit in kind is subject to withholding, at the same progressive rates and through the same monthly calculation as cash pay. There is no separate rate and no separate mechanism.
4472	Benefit
4473	Withheld?
4474	Salary in kind (food, housing, clothing as ordinary and permanent consideration)
4475	Yes, on the contractual or appraised value
4476	Company car for personal use
4477	Yes, on the value attributable to the private benefit — valued contractually, since no scale exists
4478	Employer-paid housing
4479	Yes, on the contractual value
4480	Employer-paid prepaid health / health insurance
4481	Yes, on the premium — but the employee may deduct up to 16 tax units per month
4482	Employer-paid group life or personal accident insurance
4483	Yes, where the cover is personal to the employee
4484	Non-salary-pacted benefits under art. 128
4485	Yes — the pact has no tax effect
4486	Third-party meal payments within the art. 387-1 thresholds
4487	No
4488	Third-party meal payments above 41 tax units
4489	Yes, on the excess
4490	Representation expenses, work tools, protective equipment, statutory work clothing
4491	No
4492	Employer contributions to voluntary pension funds
4493	No, within the cap
4494	
4495	The practical mechanic. A benefit in kind produces a positive earnings line with the benefit's value, which enters the taxable base, and — where the benefit is not paid in cash — an offsetting deduction line, so that net cash pay is unchanged while the withholding and, where applicable, the contributions are correctly computed. Where withholding on benefits would exceed available cash pay, see the minimum-wage protection rules in Module 4.1.
4496	Which benefits-in-kind must be processed via payroll, and which require declarations?
4497	Benefit
4498	Processed via payroll
4499	Requires an employee declaration
4500	Salary in kind — food, housing, clothing
4501	Yes, at the contractual value
4502	No — the value is in the contract
4503	Company car with private use
4504	Yes, at the attributed value
4505	No
4506	Employer-paid housing
4507	Yes
4508	No
4509	Non-salary-pacted benefits
4510	Yes, as a taxable earnings line
4511	The written pact must exist
4512	Third-party meal payments and vouchers
4513	Yes, as an informational earnings line; taxable only on the excess
4514	No — employer records
4515	Employer-paid prepaid health
4516	Yes, as a taxable earnings line
4517	Yes, to claim the 16-unit deduction: provider certificate
4518	Employer contributions to voluntary pension funds
4519	Yes, as exempt income
4520	Yes, fund certificate
4521	Employer contributions to the individual pension account
4522	Yes, as non-taxable income
4523	Yes, administrator certificate
4524	Statutory work clothing
4525	Yes, as a non-taxable, non-contributory informational line — the electronic payroll XML has a dedicated element
4526	No
4527	Representation expenses, work tools
4528	No — expense reimbursement, outside payroll
4529	Expense documentation
4530	Group life insurance
4531	Yes, where personal cover
4532	No
4533	Employee-declared reliefs (housing interest, dependants)
4534	Yes, as withholding-base adjustments
4535	Yes — see Module 7.1
4536	
4537	Reporting. The electronic payroll XML separates salary from non-salary at element level, with paired element names carrying an explicit S (salary) or NS (non-salary) suffix — salary and non-salary bonus, salary and non-salary allowance, salary and non-salary concept, salary and non-salary payment, salary and non-salary meal payment, salary and non-salary travel/accommodation allowance. The engine must therefore carry a hard salary/non-salary flag on every wage type, set at configuration, and must not attempt to derive it at reporting time.
4538	Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 128, 129 (as amended by Law 50 of 1990 arts. 15 and 16), 230; Tax Code arts. 126-1, 206, 387 lits. a) and b), 387-1, 388; Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30; Resolution DIAN 000227 of 2025, arts. 1.5.3.1.1 to 1.5.3.9.8, and its Technical Annex.
4539	7.3 Garnishments and Court Orders
4540	What are the rules for processing statutory deductions like child support, court-ordered fines, or tax levies?
4541	The controlling principle is Labour Code art. 149 num. 1: the employer "may not deduct, withhold or set off any sum from salary without an authorisation signed by the employee for each case, or without a court order." A garnishment therefore requires either a court order or, in the narrow case below, an administrative order with statutory force.
4542	Type
4543	Instrument
4544	Processing
4545	Maintenance / child support (alimentos)
4546	Judicial order from a family judge, or a conciliation agreement approved by a family commissioner or the family welfare institute
4547	Served on the employer, who becomes a retaining agent. Deduct from each payroll and remit to the beneficiary or to the court's deposit account. Up to 50% of the whole salary including the minimum-wage portion (art. 156)
4548	Judicial attachment for a civil or commercial debt (embargo)
4549	Court order in enforcement proceedings
4550	Deduct up to 20% of the excess of the NET salary over the minimum wage (art. 155). Remit to the court's deposit account
4551	Attachment for a cooperative debt
4552	Court order
4553	Up to 50% of the whole salary (art. 156)
4554	Tax levy by the tax authority
4555	Administrative enforcement (proceso de cobro coactivo) — the tax authority holds jurisdictional powers under Tax Code arts. 823 et seq. and may issue an attachment order without going to court
4556	Treated as a court order for art. 149 purposes. The attachability limits of arts. 154–156 apply
4557	Social-security enforcement by the contributions authority
4558	Administrative enforcement by the UGPP
4559	Same treatment
4560	Court-ordered fines
4561	Court order
4562	Same treatment; art. 155 limits
4563	Disciplinary sanctions
4564	Not a garnishment — an employer deduction permitted by art. 150 only where imposed under a duly approved set of work rules
4565	Subject to the minimum-wage floor
4566	
4567	Employer duties on receipt of an order.
4568	Acknowledge the order within the time it specifies and confirm the employment relationship and the
4569	salary.
4570	Apply the deduction from the next payroll falling due after service.
4571	Remit to the account the order specifies, within the period it specifies.
4572	Report any termination, unpaid leave or salary change that affects the order.
4573	Keep the order and the remittance evidence for the retention period (Module 2.3).
4574	Do not apply an order beyond its stated limit or duration, and do not apply an order served on
4575	the employee rather than the employer.
4576	Liability. Labour Code art. 149 num. 3 makes the employer "responsable de los perjuicios" for failing to make authorised, lawful deductions in good time. An employer that ignores a garnishment order can be held liable to the creditor for the amounts that should have been withheld.
4577	Is there a statutory priority order if an employee has multiple garnishments?
4578	Colombia has no single statutory garnishment-priority article, but a clear order emerges from the combination of the attachability rules, the general privilege of maintenance claims and the ordinary rules of civil enforcement. Mercans applies:
4579	Priority
4580	Claim
4581	Limit
4582	1
4583	Mandatory social security and withholding tax
4584	Statutory; applied before any garnishment
4585	2
4586	Maintenance obligations (alimentos)
4587	Up to 50% of the whole salary (art. 156). Maintenance ranks first among garnishments — the obligation is of constitutional rank (Political Constitution art. 44, the prevailing rights of the child) and art. 156 gives it the widest reach
4588	3
4589	Debts owed to cooperatives
4590	Also within the art. 156 50% reach, but ranking behind maintenance where both compete
4591	4
4592	Tax and social-security enforcement
4593	Within the art. 155 limit — 20% of the excess over the minimum wage
4594	5
4595	Ordinary civil and commercial attachments, in date order of service on the employer
4596	Within the art. 155 limit, shared among competing orders in date order
4597	6
4598	Payroll lending (libranza) and employee-authorised voluntary deductions
4599	Never above the protected floor; the aggregate must leave at least 50% of net salary (Law 1527 of 2012)
4600	
4601	Where competing orders exceed the ceiling. The employer applies orders in the priority above until the applicable ceiling is reached, then notifies the court or authority whose order cannot be satisfied, stating the competing orders and the amounts applied. The employer must not apportion pro rata on its own initiative, and must not exceed the ceiling to satisfy all orders.
4602	Interaction between the two ceilings. Art. 156 (50% of the whole salary) and art. 155 (20% of the excess over the minimum wage) are different ceilings for different claim types, not cumulative allowances. A maintenance order may reach into the minimum-wage portion; a commercial attachment may not. Where both are in play, the maintenance order is applied first within its own 50% ceiling, and the commercial attachment is then applied only to the extent that the art. 155 headroom on the excess over the minimum wage remains unused.
4603	Are there protected earnings limits?
4604	Yes — Colombia protects salary robustly, with one broad exception.
4605	Rule
4606	Protection
4607	Labour Code art. 154
4608	"Salary is exempt from attachment" — salary is not attachable as the general rule
4609	Labour Code art. 155
4610	The excess over the minimum wage is attachable only up to one fifth (20%) of that excess, computed on the NET salary after the mandatory legal deductions -- social security and withholding tax -- not on the gross (DIAN Concepto 004032 int 368 of 16 March 2026, citing Constitutional Court T-891 of 2013). So an employee on COP 3,000,000 whose net after social security is 2,760,000: the excess over 1,750,905 is 1,009,095, and the maximum attachable is 20% of that = COP 201,819. Where the net base is equal to or below the minimum wage, nothing is attachable and the deduction is zero -- never a negative figure (art. 154).
4611	Labour Code art. 156
4612	Exception: for maintenance obligations and cooperative debts, up to 50% of the whole salary, minimum wage included, is attachable. The same employee: up to COP 1,380,000 (50% of the same net salary; for maintenance the 50% is applied after the legal deductions, Law 1098 of 2006 art. 130 num. 1)
4613	Labour Code art. 149 num. 2
4614	No deduction may reduce pay below the minimum wage or into the non-attachable portion without a court order, even with written employee authorisation
4615	Law 1527 of 2012
4616	Payroll-lending deductions must leave the employee at least 50% of net salary after all statutory deductions
4617	
4618	Worked illustration. Employee on COP 3,000,000 with two orders: a maintenance order for COP 1,000,000 per month and a commercial attachment for COP 400,000 per month.
4619	Step
4620	Computation
4621	COP
4622	Gross salary
4623	
4624	3,000,000
4625	Mandatory social security (4% + 4%; base below 4 minimum wages so no solidarity fund)
4626	8% × 3,000,000
4627	(240,000)
4628	Withholding tax
4629	Depurated base well below 95 tax units
4630	0
4631	Salary available for garnishment (NET of the legal deductions)
4632	
4633	2,760,000
4634	Maintenance order, art. 156 ceiling
4635	50% × 2,760,000 = 1,380,000 → the order of 1,000,000 fits
4636	(1,000,000)
4637	Commercial attachment, art. 155 ceiling
4638	20% × (2,760,000 − 1,750,905) = 20% × 1,009,095 = 201,819 → the order of 400,000 is capped
4639	(201,819)
4640	Net pay
4641	3,000,000 − 240,000 − 1,000,000 − 201,819
4642	1,558,181
4643	Shortfall on the commercial attachment
4644	400,000 − 201,819 = 198,181
4645	Notified to the issuing court
4646	
4647	Note that both ceilings are tested against the SAME net base -- the salary after the mandatory legal deductions -- and that the resulting net pay of COP 1,558,181 is below the minimum wage — which is lawful here only because the maintenance order is a court order within the art. 156 exception. A voluntary deduction could not have produced that result.
4648	Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 149, 150, 151, 154, 155, 156; Political Constitution art. 44; Tax Code arts. 367, 368, 383 and 823 et seq.; Law 1527 of 2012; Law 1098 of 2006 (Childhood and Adolescence Code) art. 130; DIAN Concepto 004032 int 368 of 16 March 2026; Constitutional Court T-891 of 2013.

4774	
4775	Accrual methodology.
4776	Vacation accrues continuously from the first day of service at 15 working days per 360 days of
4777	service, i.e. 1.25 working days per month. The monthly employer cost accrual is 15 ÷ 360 = 4.1667% of the vacation base.
4778	Leave with a per-event trigger (sickness, maternity, paternity, bereavement, calamity) does not
4779	accrue; it arises on the event. No balance is carried.
4780	Public holidays and weekly rest are calendar entitlements, not accruals.
4781	Vacation detail — Labour Code arts. 186–192.
4782	Rule
4783	Content
4784	Art. 186 num. 1
4785	"Employees who have rendered their services for one year are entitled to fifteen (15) consecutive working days of paid holiday." Working days, not calendar days
4786	Art. 186 num. 2
4787	15 days per six months for staff in tuberculosis-control establishments and in X-ray work
4788	Art. 187
4789	The employer must set the period no later than the following year, giving 15 days' notice; num. 3 requires a special vacation register recording the entry date, the start and end of each vacation and the remuneration paid
4790	Art. 189 num. 1 (as amended by Law 1429 of 2010 art. 20)
4791	Employer and employee may agree in writing, at the employee's prior request, to pay in money up to half the vacation
4792	Art. 189 num. 3
4793	The base for the cash compensation is the last salary earned
4794	Art. 190
4795	At least 6 continuous working days per year must actually be taken and are non-accumulable; the remainder may be accumulated for up to 2 years, or 4 years for technical, specialised, trusted or management staff and for foreign nationals serving away from their family's residence
4796	Art. 192
4797	During vacation the employee receives "the ordinary salary they are earning on the day the holiday begins"; only rest-day work and supplementary overtime hours are excluded. Variable salary → the average of the immediately preceding year
4798	
4799	How are unpaid or partially paid leaves handled?
4800	Unpaid leave (licencia no remunerada) is not a statutory entitlement. It arises by agreement between employer and employee, or as a consequence of a lawful contract suspension under Labour Code art. 51 (force majeure, employer-authorised suspension of activities up to 120 days, the employee's compulsory military service, disciplinary suspension, or a preventive detention).
4801	Aspect
4802	Treatment
4803	Salary
4804	Not payable for the unpaid days. Deduct monthly salary × unpaid days ÷ 30
4805	Transport allowance
4806	Not payable for the unpaid days
4807	Contribution return
4808	Reported with the unpaid-leave change event (SLN). The contribution-return rate table shows health moving to 8.5% or 0% and pension to 12% or 16% depending on the variant, and the payroll levies falling to 0%
4809	Statutory-benefit accrual
4810	Suspended. Labour Code art. 53 provides that during suspension the employee's obligation to work and the employer's obligation to pay are suspended, but the time may be counted for severance-savings and pension purposes at the employer's option and is charged against vacation
4811	Vacation accrual
4812	Suspended for the period of unpaid leave
4813	Seniority
4814	Labour Code art. 53: the periods of suspension may be counted as service for severance savings and pensions, in which case the employer pays the corresponding contributions
4815	Withholding tax
4816	The month's taxable payments are lower; withholding follows automatically
4817	
4818	Partially paid leave in Colombia means, in substance, sickness incapacity:
4819	Period
4820	Rate
4821	Payer
4822	General illness, days 1–2
4823	66.67% (two thirds) of salary, never below the minimum wage (Labour Code art. 227; Constitutional Court C-543 of 2007; Concepto MinSalud 202311602174531 of 20 October 2023)
4824	Employer
4825	General illness, days 3–90
4826	66.67% (two thirds) of salary
4827	Health insurer
4828	General illness, days 91–180
4829	50% of salary
4830	Health insurer
4831	Occupational accident or disease, from the day after the event
4832	100% of the contribution base
4833	Occupational-risk insurer
4834	
4835	Rate for the employer's first two days. Decree 2943 of 2013 art. 1, compiled at Decree 780 of 2016 art. 3.2.1.10, allocates "the cash benefits corresponding to the first two (2)
4836	days of incapacity arising from general illness" to the employer, but does not state
4837	the rate. Labour Code art. 227 sets the cash sickness benefit (auxilio monetario) at two thirds for the first 90 days, and market practice applies 66.67% for days 1–2 on that basis. A minority practice pays 100% for those two days. Mercans default: 66.67%, configurable. Resolution: Decree 780 de 2016 art. 3.2.1.10 par. 1 assigns days 1-2 to the employer but states no rate. The Ministry of Health fills that gap by applying Labour Code art. 227 (two thirds) directly: Concepto MinSalud 202311602174531 of 20 October 2023 (request radicado 202342302183712) has the employer pay the first two days at 66.6% of salary, subject to the minimum-wage floor below. The Mercans default (66.67%, configurable) matches; the citation is added and the default is unchanged.
4838	The minimum-wage floor on incapacity payments. Where 66.67% of the employee's salary falls below the minimum wage, market practice and settled administrative doctrine top the payment up to one minimum wage. Mercans default: apply the floor, configurable. Corte Constitucional Sentencia C-543 de 2007 holds that the monetary sickness benefit (auxilio monetario por enfermedad de origen común) may never fall below the minimum legal wage -- the source the doctrine was missing. The floor is now a cited rule, not an assumed one; the Mercans default (apply the floor) was already the correct position.

4900	
4901	The essential contrast: unpaid leave suspends accrual; paid or benefit-covered leave does not.
4902	Are there any mandatory national military service leaves?
4903	Yes. Compulsory military service exists in Colombia for men, under Law 1861 of 2017 (which replaced Law 48 of 1993).
4904	Aspect
4905	Rule
4906	Contract effect
4907	Compulsory military service is a cause of suspension of the employment contract — Labour Code art. 51 num. 4: "the employee being called up to perform military service"
4908	Salary
4909	Not payable during the suspension. This is an unpaid statutory suspension, not paid leave
4910	Reinstatement
4911	Labour Code art. 51 num. 4 obliges the employer to keep the post open and to reinstate the employee on discharge. Law 1861 of 2017 art. 42 reinforces the guarantee
4912	Time to present
4913	The employee must present for reinstatement within 30 days of discharge
4914	Seniority
4915	The period counts for seniority purposes on reinstatement
4916	Social security
4917	Contributions are suspended with the contract; the conscript is covered by the military health system during service
4918	Contribution return
4919	Reported as a suspension change event (SLN) for the affected periods, or as a termination and rehire where the service is long
4920	Reserve call-up / training
4921	Short-term reserve obligations are treated as leave for a compulsory transitory public office under Labour Code art. 57 num. 6 lit. b) — paid
4922	
4923	Not a statutory leave in the accrual sense. Military service does not accrue, does not carry a balance and does not generate an employer payment. The engine must model it as a long unpaid suspension with a reinstatement guarantee.
4924	Are any other kinds of leave paid by government authorities?
4925	Yes — this is a defining feature of Colombian leave. The employer pays only a narrow set of short paid absences; the substantial paid leaves are borne by the social-security institutions.
4926	Leave
4927	Paid by
4928	Rate
4929	Employer's role
4930	General-illness incapacity, days 1–2
4931	Employer
4932	66.67%, never below the minimum wage (Constitutional Court C-543 of 2007)
4933	Pays directly
4934	General-illness incapacity, from day 3
4935	Health insurer (EPS)
4936	66.67% to day 90; 50% days 91–180
4937	Typically advances the payment through payroll and recovers from the insurer
4938	General-illness incapacity, days 181–540
4939	Pension administrator (AFP), where the EPS issued a favourable rehabilitation concept; if the EPS did not issue the concept (due before day 120), the EPS pays from its own resources until it does (Law 100 of 1993 art. 41)
4940	Equal to the benefit being paid at day 180 – 'subsidio equivalente a la incapacidad que venía disfrutando' (Law 100 of 1993 art. 41); under Labour Code art. 227 that is 50% of salary, never below the monthly minimum wage (Constitutional Court C-543 of 2007)
4941	Keeps reporting the PILA novelty; health and pension contributions continue on the benefit value
4942	General-illness incapacity, from day 541
4943	Health insurer (EPS), funded by ADRES (Decree 780 of 2016 art. 2.2.3.6.1; Law 1753 of 2015 art. 67)
4944	The amount the EPS recognises and pays to the employer; payment rests on Decree 780 of 2016 art. 2.2.3.6.1, and the EPS reviews and settles the claim within 15 working days of its submission (art. 2.2.3.4.3); no statutory rate fixed
4945	Same as above
4946	Maternity leave — 18 weeks
4947	Health insurer
4948	Salary earned when the leave begins; variable salary → average of the last year
4949	Advances and recovers
4950	Paternity leave — 2 weeks
4951	Health insurer
4952	Full salary
4953	Advances and recovers
4954	Shared and flexible parental leave
4955	Health insurer
4956	Salary of whoever takes the leave
4957	Advances and recovers
4958	Occupational accident or disease incapacity
4959	Occupational-risk insurer (ARL), from the day after the event
4960	100% of the contribution base
4961	Advances and recovers
4962	Invalidity and survivor pensions
4963	Pension administrator
4964	Per the pension rules
4965	None
4966	Family subsidy (subsidio familiar)
4967	Family compensation fund
4968	Per the fund's scale
4969	Reports eligible dependants
4970	Bereavement, voting, calamity, medical appointments, school obligations, summonses, bicycle rest day
4971	Employer
4972	Full salary
4973	Pays directly
4974	Vacation
4975	Employer
4976	Ordinary salary
4977	Pays directly
4978	Incapacity beyond 180 days. Days 181–540 are paid by the pension administrator as a benefit equal to the one the employee was receiving, where the EPS issued a favourable rehabilitation concept; if it did not, the EPS pays until it does (Law 100 of 1993 art. 41, as amended by Decree-Law 019 of 2012 art. 142). From day 541 the health insurer pays, funded by ADRES (Law 1753 of 2015 art. 67), in the cases of Decree 780 of 2016 art. 2.2.3.6.1 (as substituted by Decree 2126 of 2023), liquidating the benefit and paying it to the employer (art. 2.2.3.4.3). The benefit is computed on the contribution base reported for the month before the initial incapacity (art. 2.2.3.3.1); increases above 40% of the 12-month average are disregarded (art. 2.2.3.4.5).
4979	Payroll consequences. Health and pension contributions continue throughout on a base equal to the value of the incapacity, never below the statutory minimum base (Decree 780 of 2016 art. 3.2.1.10); the Tax Code art. 114-1 exoneration applies as usual; no occupational-risk contribution accrues (Decree 1072 of 2015 art. 2.2.4.2.1.6). The benefit is taxable (DIAN Oficio 27408 of 2019, item 7.1), not exempt under Tax Code art. 206 num. 1; the general 25% exemption of num. 10 still applies. The contract is not suspended, because incapacity is not among the Labour Code art. 51 causes (Supreme Court SL588-2024, reiterated in SL1659-2025): the days count for the service bonus, severance savings and vacation, but the benefit amount does not enter the salary average for those items (Supreme Court SL1659-2025, instance ruling).
4980	
4981	The cash-flow mechanic. Colombian employers customarily advance the benefit through payroll and recover from the health or risk insurer, because the institution pays in arrears and the employee cannot wait. The engine must therefore support a benefit line that is paid to the employee but receivable from the institution, and must not treat it as an employer cost.
4982	Documentation for the paternity benefit is exacting. Labour Code art. 236 par. 2 requires the civil birth registration as the only valid supporting document, to be filed with the health insurer within 30 days of the birth. The benefit is recognised in proportion to the weeks the father contributed during gestation.
4983	What are the payout rules for statutory leaves?
4984	Leave
4985	Payout rule
4986	Vacation
4987	Paid at the ordinary salary being earned on the day the leave begins. Excludes rest-day work and supplementary overtime hours only; the night premium and commissions stay in. Variable salary → the average of the immediately preceding year. The transport allowance is not part of the vacation base — it compensates travel that does not occur during leave
4988	Cash compensation of vacation
4989	Up to half the entitlement, on the employee's prior written request, at the last salary earned (Labour Code art. 189 nums. 1 and 3)
4990	Public holidays and weekly rest
4991	One day's ordinary salary, "but without any deduction being made for absence from work" (art. 177). Where the agreed schedule does not require work on all working days of the week, the rest day is paid in proportion to time worked (art. 173 num. 5)
4992	General-illness incapacity
4993	66.67% for days 1–90; 50% for days 91–180. Variable salary → the average of the year of service preceding the incapacity, or of the whole period if under a year (art. 228)
4994	Occupational incapacity
4995	100% of the contribution base, from the day after the event
4996	Maternity and paternity
4997	Salary earned when the leave begins; variable salary → average of the last year
4998	Bereavement, voting, calamity, medical appointments, summonses
4999	Full ordinary salary; no deduction
5000	
5001	A structural point on the paid rest day. Labour Code art. 174 num. 2 provides that "Every salary is understood to include payment for rest on the days on which rest is legally mandatory and paid." For a monthly-salaried employee the paid rest day is already inside the monthly salary and is not a separate payment. For a daily-rated employee it is a distinct payment. The engine must not double-pay the monthly-salaried case.
5002	How are unused paid leaves treated upon termination?
5003	Leave
5004	Treatment on termination
5005	Vacation
5006	Paid out in full, in cash, in proportion to the time worked. This is unconditional — it applies whatever the reason for termination, including dismissal for cause and employee resignation. The 6-day non-accumulable minimum of art. 190 does not bar the payout; it constrains in-service accumulation only
5007	Accumulated vacation from prior years
5008	Paid out in full
5009	Service bonus
5010	Paid pro rata for the semester worked to the termination date
5011	Severance savings
5012	Liquidated and paid to the employee on termination (not deposited to the fund)
5013	Interest on severance savings
5014	Paid to the employee, proportional to the elapsed part of the year
5015	Sickness incapacity in progress
5016	Continues to be paid by the health insurer to the extent of the certified incapacity; a dismissal during certified incapacity carries a separate protection-against-dismissal risk
5017	Public holidays, weekly rest, per-event leaves
5018	No balance; nothing to pay out
5019	Unpaid leave
5020	No balance
5021	
5022	Vacation payout formula and worked example.
5023	Vacation payout = base salary (ex overtime and rest-day work)                  x days of service in the accrual period                  / 720
5024	The 720 divisor derives from 15 working days per 360 days of service, expressed in monthly-salary terms: 15 ÷ 30 = 0.5 months of salary per 360 days, so salary × days ÷ 720.
5025	Example. Employee on COP 2,400,000 (no overtime, no rest-day work), terminating on 31 August 2026 with 510 days of service since the last vacation settlement:
5026	2,400,000 × 510 ÷ 720 = COP 1,700,000
5027	Tax on the payout. Vacation compensation is ordinary taxable employment income within the monthly withholding base. It is not salary, so it does not enter the health, pension and occupational-risk contribution base (Consejo de Estado, Sección Cuarta, exp. 26941 of 4 April 2024; unification ruling exp. 25185 of 9 December 2021, rule 1); it does enter the payroll-levy base for the family compensation fund, SENA and ICBF, because Law 21 of 1982 art. 17 covers paid rest whether taken or paid in cash (Law 89 of 1988 art. 1 par. 1; Consejo de Estado, Sala de Consulta, concept 2027 of 4 October 2010) — a point frequently missed on termination settlements.
5028	Provide the calculation logic for each leave type where applicable.
5029	Let S = monthly salary, T = transport allowance (COP 249,095 where due), d = days.
5030	Leave
5031	Formula
5032	Vacation accrual
5033	S_vacation_base × d ÷ 720, where the base excludes overtime and rest-day work
5034	Vacation taken
5035	S_vacation_base × working days of leave ÷ 30
5036	Vacation cash compensation
5037	last salary × days compensated ÷ 720, maximum half the entitlement
5038	Vacation payout on termination
5039	S_vacation_base × days of service ÷ 720
5040	Sickness, days 1–2 (employer)
5041	S ÷ 30 × 2/3 × d, floored at the minimum wage equivalent
5042	Sickness, days 3–90 (health insurer)
5043	contribution base ÷ 30 × 2/3 × d, floored at the minimum wage equivalent
5044	Sickness, days 91–180 (health insurer)
5045	contribution base ÷ 30 × 0.50 × d, floored at the minimum wage equivalent
5046	Occupational incapacity
5047	contribution base ÷ 30 × 1.00 × d, from the day after the event
5048	Maternity, 18 weeks = 126 calendar days
5049	S ÷ 30 × 126
5050	Paternity, 2 weeks = 14 calendar days
5051	S ÷ 30 × 14
5052	Bereavement, 5 working days
5053	S ÷ 30 × 5
5054	Public holiday
5055	Already inside a monthly salary (art. 174 num. 2); S ÷ 30 × 1 for a daily-rated employee
5056	Unpaid leave deduction
5057	−(S + T) ÷ 30 × d
5058	
5059	Worked example — a mixed month. September 2026. Employee on COP 2,400,000 plus the transport allowance, on COP 2,200,000 through 31 August 2026 before a raise to the stated COP 2,400,000 effective 1 September, who takes 5 working days of vacation, is certified sick for 4 days (days 1–2 employer, days 3–4 health insurer), and works the remaining days.
5060	Line
5061	Computation
5062	COP
5063	Salary for days worked (30 − 5 vacation − 4 sick = 21 days)
5064	2,400,000 × 21 ÷ 30
5065	1,680,000.00
5066	Vacation pay, 5 working days
5067	2,400,000 × 5 ÷ 30
5068	400,000.00
5069	Sickness, days 1–2, employer, 2/3 floored at the minimum wage
5070	max(2,400,000 ÷ 30 × 2/3 ; 1,750,905 ÷ 30) × 2
5071	116,727.00
5072	Sickness, days 3–4, health insurer, 2/3 floored at the minimum wage (advanced by the employer, recoverable)
5073	max(2,400,000 ÷ 30 × 2/3 ; 1,750,905 ÷ 30) × 2
5074	116,727.00
5075	Transport allowance, days actually worked and on vacation — not for sick days
5076	249,095 × 26 ÷ 30
5077	215,882.33
5078	Total gross earnings
5079	
5080	2,529,336.33
5081	Contribution base
5082	Salary 1,680,000 (September rate, days worked) + vacation-days IBC 366,666.67 (2,200,000 × 5 ÷ 30: August's IBC - the last IBC reported before the vacation began, Decreto 780 de 2016 art. 3.2.5.1, NOT the 400,000 actually paid) + sickness 233,454.00 (contributions continue during incapacity; IBC = value of the incapacity, floored at the minimum wage, art. 3.2.1.10); transport allowance excluded; 2,280,120.67 rounded up to the whole peso (Decreto 780 de 2016 art. 3.2.1.5, as modified by Decreto 1990 de 2016 art. 1)
5083	2,280,121
5084	Employee health 4%
5085	
5086	91,300 (up to the nearest 100)
5087	Employee pension 4%
5088	
5089	91,300
5090	Solidarity fund
5091	Base < 4 minimum wages
5092	0
5093	Net pay
5094	2,529,336.33 − 182,600
5095	2,346,736.33
5096	
5097	Note the three behaviours the example demonstrates: vacation days enter the contribution base on the PRIOR month's IBC, not the current month's (Art. 3.2.5.1 Decreto 780 de 2016) -- the employee is still PAID at the current rate, but the health/pension base for those days looks back one month, and contributions continue during incapacity, while the transport allowance is excluded from the base and is not paid for sick days. Occupational-risk (ARL) contributions are not due for the vacation days: vacation is a reportable change event (novedad) during which no employer ARL contribution accrues (Decreto 1072 de 2015 art. 2.2.4.2.1.6 num. 1.3, compiling Decreto 1772 de 1994 art. 19 as amended by Decreto 1528 de 2015). A Función Pública concept addressed to a public entity (Concepto 091961 de 2022) takes the contrary view without addressing that article; it is not binding (CPACA art. 28).
5098	How does extended unpaid leave impact social security contributions, tax status, and accrual?
5099	Dimension
5100	Effect of extended unpaid leave
5101	Employment relationship
5102	Suspended, not terminated. Labour Code art. 53: the employee's obligation to work and the employer's obligation to pay are suspended
5103	Health contribution
5104	Reported with the unpaid-leave change event (SLN). The contribution-return rate table shows the health rate moving to 8.5% or 0% depending on the variant. In the 8.5% variant, the employer bears the whole health contribution alone to preserve the employee's health cover
5105	Pension contribution
5106	Rate of 12% or 16% depending on the variant; a public-law employer may contribute 75% of the total rate
5107	Occupational risk
5108	Not payable — there is no exposure while the contract is suspended
5109	Payroll levies
5110	Fall to 0% for the family compensation fund, the apprenticeship training levy and the family welfare levy
5111	Health cover for the employee
5112	Preserved where the employer maintains the health contribution. Extended unpaid leave without contribution risks loss of health cover, and the employer bears the consequence where the suspension was employer-initiated
5113	Tax status
5114	No change to residence or to the withholding procedure. Taxable payments are simply lower or nil for the period. Under Procedure 2 the fixed percentage is unchanged, so a long unpaid absence produces mechanical over-withholding on resumption until the next semi-annual recalculation
5115	Vacation accrual
5116	Suspended
5117	Service-bonus accrual
5118	Suspended — art. 306 accrues on time worked
5119	Severance-savings accrual
5120	Suspended, unless the employer elects under art. 53 to count the period, in which case it pays the corresponding contributions
5121	Seniority for the dismissal indemnity
5122	Follows the same art. 53 election
5123	Return to work
5124	The employee resumes on the same terms. Where the suspension exceeded 120 days for an employer-authorised suspension of activities, the limit of art. 51 num. 3 has been breached and the suspension is unlawful
5125	Contribution-return mechanics
5126	The SLN change event carries the affected day count; the base is reduced pro rata for partial-month suspensions
5127	
5128	Configuration rule. Model extended unpaid leave as a suspension with an accrual switch, not as a zero-pay month. The engine must (a) stop the four benefit accruals, (b) report the SLN change event with the correct day count, (c) apply the SLN rate variant the client has elected, and (d) hold the art. 53 seniority election as a per-case flag.
5129	Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 51, 53, 57 num. 6 and num. 10, 172, 173, 174, 177, 186, 187, 189, 190, 192, 227, 228, 230, 232, 236 (as amended by Law 2114 of 2021 art. 2), 249, 306 (as amended by Law 1788 of 2016 art. 1) and art. 57 as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 15; Law 1280 of 2009 art. 1; Law 1429 of 2010 art. 20; Law 1861 of 2017; Law 2338 of 2023; Law 776 of 2002 art. 3; Decree 2943 of 2013 art. 1 (compiled at Decree 780 of 2016 art. 3.2.1.10); Tax Code art. 206 nums. 1–2 and par. 1; Resolution MSPS 2388 of 2016 Technical Annex 2.
5130	
5131	Module 9: Termination & Irregular Payments
5132	Objective: To detail calculation and tax rules for non-standard payments.
5133	9.1 Termination Payments
5134	What are the different types of termination payments?
5135	A Colombian termination settlement (liquidación de prestaciones sociales) is made up of two families of payment: accrued entitlements, which are always due whatever the reason for termination, and indemnities, which are due only in defined circumstances.
5136	Family A — accrued entitlements. Always payable, whatever the reason for termination, including dismissal for cause and employee resignation.
5137	#
5138	Payment
5139	Basis
5140	Legal source
5141	A1
5142	Salary to the termination date
5143	(S + T) × days ÷ 30
5144	Labour Code art. 127
5145	A2
5146	Severance savings (cesantías)
5147	One month's salary per year of service and pro rata for a fraction of a year
5148	Labour Code art. 249
5149	A3
5150	Interest on severance savings
5151	12% per year on the balance, proportional to the elapsed part of the year
5152	Law 52 of 1975 art. 1
5153	A4
5154	Service bonus (prima de servicios)
5155	15 days per semester, pro rata to the termination date
5156	Labour Code art. 306
5157	A5
5158	Unused vacation
5159	15 working days per year of service, pro rata
5160	Labour Code arts. 186, 189
5161	A6
5162	Overtime, premiums and commissions earned but unpaid
5163	As earned
5164	Labour Code arts. 127, 134
5165	A7
5166	Work clothing owed but not delivered
5167	Value of the undelivered items
5168	Labour Code arts. 230, 232

5171	#
5172	Payment

5174	Legal source

5176	Indemnity for dismissal without just cause
5177	Employer terminates without a just cause listed in art. 62
5178	Labour Code art. 64 (as amended by Law 789 of 2002 art. 28)
5179	B2
5180	Late-payment penalty (indemnización moratoria)
5181	Salaries and statutory benefits unpaid at termination
5182	Labour Code art. 65 (as amended by Law 789 of 2002 art. 29)
5183	B3
5184	Penalty for non-deposit of severance savings
5185	The employer failed to deposit severance savings to the fund by 14 February
5186	Law 50 of 1990 art. 99 num. 3
5187	B4
5188	Penalty for non-payment of severance interest
5189	Interest unpaid in January
5190	Law 52 of 1975 art. 1 num. 3
5191	B5
5192	Indemnity for dismissal during protected status
5193	Dismissal of a pregnant or nursing employee, an employee on certified incapacity, an employee with a disability, or an employee with trade-union immunity — requires prior authorisation from a labour inspector or a judge
5194	Labour Code art. 239; Law 361 of 1997 art. 26
5195	B6
5196	Payment in lieu of notice
5197	Only where the contract or a collective agreement provides for it — there is no statutory notice period for the employer
5198	Contractual
5199	B7

5201	By agreement
5202	Contractual
5203	B8
5204	Pension bonus (bono pensional)
5205	On transfer between pension regimes; not an employer payroll payment
5206	Law 100 of 1993
5207	
5208	Not a termination payment: the employee's own 30-day notice on resignation from an indefinite-term contract (Labour Code art. 47, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 5). That article expressly forbids any penalty on an employee who omits the notice: "In no case may a penalty be agreed for an employee who omits the notice described here."
5209	How do termination rules and calculations differ by residential status and employer type?
5210	By residence status: the labour-law entitlements are identical; only the tax treatment differs.
5211	Aspect
5212	Resident
5213	Non-resident
5214	Severance savings, interest, service bonus, vacation
5215	Identical — the Labour Code applies to every employee working in Colombia, whatever their nationality or tax residence (Labour Code art. 2)
5216	Identical
5217	Dismissal indemnity
5218	Identical
5219	Identical
5220	Tax on severance savings and interest
5221	Exempt in full where the average monthly income of the last 6 months ≤ 350 tax units (COP 18,330,900); tapered above
5222	Flat 20% on the gross under Tax Code art. 408 — the art. 206 num. 4 exemption does not attach to a non-resident's flat withholding
5223	Tax on the dismissal indemnity
5224	Fully taxable — withheld outside the ordinary monthly base: 20% where income exceeds 204 tax units, otherwise the table percentage for the 12-month average (Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.8)
5225	Flat 20%
5226	Tax on accrued vacation and service bonus
5227	Ordinary treatment
5228	Flat 20%
5229	Social security on the settlement
5230	Accrued vacation paid on termination is not in the health, pension and occupational-risk base (Consejo de Estado, Sección Cuarta, exp. 26941 of 4 April 2024; unification ruling exp. 25185 of 9 December 2021, rule 1) but enters the payroll-levy base (Law 21 of 1982 art. 17); the other accrued entitlements and the indemnity are in neither
5231	Identical
5232	
5233	By employer type.
5234	Employer type
5235	Difference
5236	Legal person, income-tax filer
5237	Standard. Qualifies for the Tax Code art. 114-1 exemption
5238	Natural-person employer with 2 or more employees
5239	Standard. Qualifies for the exemption
5240	Natural-person employer with fewer than 2 employees
5241	No exemption — art. 114-1 inciso 2 excludes them: "The foregoing shall not apply to natural persons who employ fewer than two employees"
5242	Taxpayer at the special rate of Tax Code art. 240 par. 8
5243	No exemption — art. 114-1 par. 6, added by Law 2133 of 2021 art. 31
5244	Public higher-education institutions
5245	No apprenticeship training levy — art. 114-1 par. 5
5246	Temporary employment agency
5247	The agency is the employer of the assigned worker and owes the full settlement. Law 2466 of 2025 art. 45 adds that where the statutory limits on agency use are breached, the user undertaking is treated as the true employer and the agency as a mere intermediary, on a prior judicial declaration
5248	Contractors and subcontractors
5249	Labour Code art. 34, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 44 — the principal is jointly and severally liable for the contractor's labour obligations where the work is within the principal's ordinary line of business
5250	
5251	By contract type — this changes the indemnity, not the accrued entitlements.
5252	Contract type
5253	Indemnity for dismissal without just cause
5254	Indefinite term
5255	Tiered by salary and seniority — see the table below
5256	Fixed term (maximum 4 years under Labour Code art. 46 as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 6)
5257	The salaries corresponding to the remaining term
5258	Specific work or task (obra o labor)
5259	The remaining term of the work, never less than 15 days
5260	Apprenticeship
5261	A special fixed-term labour contract governed by the Labour Code (art. 81, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 21); in the practical or dual phase the apprentice is entitled to "all the benefits, allowances and other rights proper to an employment contract"
5262	
5263	Detail the calculation and tax withholding rules for unused leave paid out on termination.
5264	Calculation.
5265	Vacation payout = vacation base × days of service since the last settlement ÷ 720where vacation base = the ordinary salary being earned, EXCLUDING                      rest-day work and supplementary overtime hours only                      (the night premium and commissions stay IN)                      and EXCLUDING the transport allowanceVariable salary     -> average of the immediately preceding year (art. 192 num. 2)
5266	The 720 divisor derives from 15 working days per 360 days of service expressed in monthly-salary terms: 15 ÷ 30 = 0.5 months per 360 days.
5267	Tax withholding.
5268	Aspect
5269	Treatment
5270	Taxable?
5271	Yes, fully. Vacation compensation is ordinary taxable employment income. There is no exemption for it, and it does not carry the art. 206 num. 4 severance-savings exemption
5272	Which base?
5273	The ordinary monthly withholding base of the termination month, under Procedure 1 or Procedure 2
5274	Separate table read?
5275	No. Only the statutory minimum service bonus gets a separate read, and only under Procedure 1
5276	25% exempt-income deduction?
5277	Yes, it participates in the ordinary depuration within the 40% and 790-unit caps
5278	Social security
5279	Not in the health, pension and occupational-risk contribution base: vacation compensation is not salary (Consejo de Estado, Sección Cuarta, exp. 26941 of 4 April 2024; unification ruling exp. 25185 of 9 December 2021, rule 1). It does enter the payroll-levy base (family compensation fund, SENA, ICBF: Law 21 of 1982 art. 17; Law 89 of 1988 art. 1 par. 1). The payroll-levy line is the one most frequently missed on a Colombian termination settlement
5280	Non-resident
5281	Flat 20% on the gross under art. 408
5282	
5283	Describe the rules for statutory severance/redundancy payments.
5284	Colombia has no redundancy regime as such. There is no economic-dismissal procedure, no collective redundancy consultation for the ordinary case, and no separate redundancy payment. A dismissal for economic reasons is simply a dismissal without just cause, compensated by the art. 64 indemnity.
5285	What Colombia does have instead is severance savings — a different instrument entirely.
5286	(a) Severance savings (auxilio de cesantía) — Labour Code art. 249.
5287	"Every employer is obliged to pay its employees … on termination of the employment contract, as severance savings (auxilio de cesantía), one month of salary for each year of service and proportionally for any fraction of a year."
5288	The transport allowance is included in this base and in the severance-interest base, even though it is not salary -- Ley 1 de 1963 art. 7 incorporates it into the base "para todos los efectos de liquidacion de prestaciones sociales" (for all effects of liquidating social benefits). This is a different treatment from vacation pay and from the social-security/parafiscal base, both of which exclude it -- the same allowance figure cannot be reused across those three calculations.
5289	WARNING for any automated severance-deposit build: the engine must compute the cesantías base itself, as monthly salary plus the transport allowance where it is due (Ley 1 de 1963 art. 7; Ley 2466 de 2025 art. 64), and must not copy a figure from any operator or portal calculator. Leaving out the allowance understates the annual deposit for a minimum-wage employee by one month of allowance (COP 249,095 for 2026). A late or short deposit costs one day's salary per day of delay (Ley 50 de 1990 art. 99 num. 3; the statute sets no cap). Ley 2466 de 2025 art. 64 adds two OPTIONAL mechanisms to art. 99. (i) Monthly payment of the 1% cesantías interest directly to the employee: needs a WRITTEN agreement between the parties (par. 2) and is operable today. (ii) A monthly 8.33% advance deposit to the fund (par. 1): depends on MinSalud's PILA regulation, which is not in PILA Anexo Técnico 2 v30 (24-07-2026), and on the MinTrabajo implementing decree, still a draft. Until then the annual deposit under art. 99 num. 3 (before 15 February) remains the operable route.
5290	Element
5291	Rule
5292	Entitlement
5293	One month's salary per year of service, pro rata for a fraction
5294	Base
5295	Labour Code art. 253: the last monthly salary, provided it has not varied in the last 3 months; otherwise, and for variable salaries, the average of the last year of service, or of the whole period if shorter. The base includes the transport allowance
5296	Annual regime (Law 50 of 1990 art. 99, for all employees hired from 1 January 1991)
5297	Liquidated at 31 December each year and deposited to the employee's chosen fund before 15 February (i.e. by 14 February). Late deposit penalty: one day's salary for each day of delay
5298	Monthly advance deposit — new
5299	Law 2466 of 2025 art. 64 adds a paragraph to Law 50 of 1990 art. 99 permitting the employer to deposit 8.33% of the base monthly as an advance deposit. This is not a partial payment of severance savings — the employee can access the money only in the cases the law allows or on termination
5300	Monthly interest — new
5301	The same article permits the parties to agree in writing to pay the interest monthly, at 1% of the base
5302	Direct payment to the employee
5303	Labour Code art. 57 par. 4, added by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 15: where the employer proves direct payment to the employee, that payment is discharging and no non-deposit penalty applies, unless it is shown that the payment was not made freely or was not applied to a legally authorised purpose. Affiliation to a severance-savings fund nonetheless remains compulsory
5304	Partial payments
5305	Labour Code art. 254 prohibits partial payment before termination except in the expressly authorised cases (housing acquisition, improvement or debt release; education of the employee, spouse or children), on pain of losing the sums paid
5306	Loss of the right — art. 250
5307	Delictual acts against the employer or the employer's relatives; intentional grave material damage to buildings, works, machinery or raw materials; disclosure of technical or commercial secrets causing grave prejudice. The employer may withhold payment until a court decides
5308	Pre-1991 regime
5309	Employees hired before 1 January 1991 who did not migrate remain on the retrospective annual-adjustment regime. Reported as a separate line on the information return
5310	Construction workers — art. 310
5311	Special regime: 3 days' salary per completed month
5312	All-inclusive salary
5313	Absorbed
5314	
5315	(b) Interest on severance savings — Law 52 of 1975 art. 1.
5316	"… shall recognise and pay interest of 12% per annum on the balances that, at 31 December of each year, or on the dates of the employee's departure or of a partial settlement of severance savings, the employee holds to their credit in respect of severance savings. … The interest … must be paid in the month of January of the following year … or on the date of the employee's departure … in an amount proportional to the part of the year elapsed. … If the employer fails to pay the employee the interest established here … it must pay the wage earner, by way of indemnity and on one occasion only, the amount [equal to the interest owed]."
5317	The interest is paid to the employee directly, not to the fund — unlike the principal.
5318	(c) Indemnity for dismissal without just cause — Labour Code art. 64.
5319	Employee's salary
5320	≤ 1 year of service
5321	Each subsequent year and pro rata
5322	Below 10 minimum wages (< COP 17,509,050)
5323	30 days' salary
5324	+20 days' salary
5325	10 minimum wages or above
5326	20 days' salary
5327	+15 days' salary
5328	
5329	Transitional paragraph: employees with 10 or more years of continuous service at the entry into force of Law 789 of 2002 retain the earlier Law 50 of 1990 art. 6 literals b), c) and d) scale.
5330	(d) The social-security clearance condition — Labour Code art. 65 par. 1. An easily missed gate:
5331	"In order to proceed with the termination of the employment contract …, the employer must inform the employee in writing, at the last registered address, within the sixty (60) days following the termination, of the payment status of the social security and payroll-levy contributions on the salaries of the last three months preceding the termination, attaching the payment receipts … If the employer does not prove payment of those contributions, the termination of the contract shall have no effect. However, the employer may pay the contributions during the following sixty (60) days, with default interest."
5332	(e) Statement of cause — Labour Code art. 66. The terminating party must state the cause at the moment of termination; different causes may not validly be alleged afterwards.
5333	Provide the statutory formula for any tax-free portion.
5334	The tax-free portion attaches to the severance savings and their interest, and to nothing else.
5335	Tax Code art. 206 num. 4:
5336	Determine A = the average MONTHLY income over the LAST SIX MONTHS of employment,              expressed in tax units (1 unit = COP 52,374 in 2026)If A <= 350 tax units (COP 18,330,900):    severance savings and interest are EXEMPT IN FULLOtherwise apply the taper:

5363	Yes — the taper above
5364	Service bonus
5365	None
5366	Unused vacation
5367	None
5368	Salary to the termination date
5369	None
5370	Dismissal indemnity
5371	None in the general case. Tax Code art. 206 nums. 1–3 exempt accident, illness and maternity-protection indemnities, and art. 206 par. 1 limits even those to the statutory minimum amount: "operates only on the amounts corresponding to the statutory minimum … the excess is not exempt". A dismissal indemnity under art. 64 is not within nums. 1–3 and is fully taxable
5372	Late-payment penalty
5373	None
5374	Negotiated settlement bonus
5375	None
5376	
5377	Also excluded from the base entirely. Severance savings and their interest are excluded from the monthly withholding base under both Procedure 1 and Procedure 2 (Tax Code arts. 385 and 386). They are not simply exempt — they never enter the monthly calculation at all, and the art. 206 num. 4 exemption is applied when the payment itself is made. Also, the 25% exemption of art. 206 num. 10 does not apply to severance savings (art. 206 par. 2).

5494	Deliverable: Detail the rules for any applicable concessional tax cap
5495	Not Applicable — Colombia has no concessional tax cap on termination payments.
5496	There is no equivalent of a lifetime or per-event cap on concessionally taxed termination payments, no tax-free redundancy allowance and no reduced rate for termination income. The only concessional treatment in the Colombian termination settlement is the art. 206 num. 4 exemption on severance savings and their interest, which is:
5497	income-tested, not capped by amount — the test is the average monthly income of the last six months
5498	of employment, and the exemption tapers from 100% to 0% across the 350-to-650-tax-unit range;
5499	not a lifetime allowance — it applies to each termination on its own facts;
5500	not extendable to any other termination payment.
5501	Three related caps exist and must not be confused with a concessional termination cap:
5502	Cap
5503	What it limits
5504	Applies to termination?
5505	790 tax units per year on the 25% exempt income (art. 206 num. 10)
5506	The general labour-income exemption
5507	Yes, to the ordinary monthly base of the termination month — but not to severance savings (art. 206 par. 2)
5508	1,340 tax units per year on combined deductions and exempt income (art. 336 num. 3)
5509	The total of deductions and exemptions
5510	Yes, to the ordinary base
5511	40% of the post-non-taxable-income figure (art. 388)
5512	The same total
5513	Yes, to the ordinary base
5514	
5515	Also not applicable: there is no spreading and no top-slicing. The dismissal indemnity is withheld outside the ordinary monthly base: 20% where the employee's income exceeds 204 tax units (Tax Code art. 401-3), otherwise the table percentage for the average monthly income of the previous 12 months (Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.8).
5516	Deliverable: Provide a detailed worked example of a redundancy termination payout
5517	Facts. Employee terminated without just cause on 31 August 2026 for economic reasons — in Colombian law simply a dismissal without just cause, since no redundancy regime exists.

5622	Step 7 — Social security on the settlement
5623	Only the August salary is contributory to health, pension and occupational risk. The vacation payout is not salary and stays out of that base (Consejo de Estado, Sección Cuarta, exp. 26941 of 4 April 2024; unification ruling exp. 25185 of 9 December 2021, rule 1), but it enters the payroll-levy base (Law 21 of 1982 art. 17; Step 10). The service bonus, severance savings, interest and the indemnity are in neither base.

Premises (operating readings, not stated in the cited provisions): the 204-tax-unit test is applied to the monthly income (4,500,000 = 85.92 tax units); the final settlement paid on termination (vacation payout and service bonus) is not counted among the payments of the 12 months before the termination date. If it were counted, the average and the percentage would have to be recomputed.

5808	9.2 Irregular and Retrospective Payments
5809	Describe the specific tax calculation method required for irregular payments.
5810	Colombia has no special method for irregular payments. There is no averaging, no spreading, no top-slicing, no annualisation and no separate schedule, except that a dismissal indemnity or retirement bonus is withheld outside the monthly base (20% above 204 tax units under Tax Code art. 401-3; otherwise the table percentage on the 12-month average under Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.1.8). An irregular payment is added to the taxable payments of the month in which it is paid, and the ordinary calculation runs.
5811	The four exceptions, all of which are rules about particular payments, not about irregularity as such:
5812	Exception
5813	Rule
5814	Statutory minimum service bonus, Procedure 1
5815	Separate read of the art. 383 table on the bonus alone (art. 385)
5816	Severance savings and interest
5817	Excluded from the monthly base under both procedures; art. 206 num. 4 exemption and taper applied on payment
5818	Procedure 2 generally
5819	The fixed percentage smooths irregular payments across the semester by construction — the payment raises the base the percentage is applied to but does not change the percentage until the next June or December recalculation
5820	Sub-30-day periods, Procedure 1
5821	Gross to 30 days, read the table percentage, apply that percentage to the actual payment (art. 385)
5822	
5823	The consequence, stated plainly. Under Procedure 1 a large irregular payment in a single month pushes the depurated base into a higher marginal band for that month only, producing a spike in withholding. That is arithmetically correct — the employee did receive that income in that month — and it is reconciled in the employee's own annual return, not by the employer. Under Procedure 2 the same payment produces a smaller spike, because the fixed percentage is unchanged.
5824	Two caps bite hardest on irregular payments, and the engine must apply them monthly:
5825	Cap
5826	Apportioned monthly figure 2026 (operating convention)
5827	790 tax units per year on the 25% exempt income
5828	65.8333 units = COP 3,447,955
5829	1,340 tax units per year on combined deductions and exempt income
5830	111.6667 units = COP 5,848,430
5831	
5832	A one-off payment large enough to make 25% of the depurated base exceed COP 3,447,955 will hit the first cap — as demonstrated in the retention-bonus example in Module 6.3.
5833	Social security on irregular payments. An irregular payment that is salary enters the contribution base of the month it is paid, subject to the 25-minimum-wage ceiling and, where a non-salary pact applies, to the 40% clamp. A large one-off payment is therefore commonly the month in which the ceiling or the clamp bites for the first time.
5834	How are backdated payments and retrospective salary adjustments handled?
5835	The controlling principle is that Colombian payroll is settled on a payment basis, not an accrual basis, for tax and social security alike. A retrospective payment is generally taxed and contributed in the month it is paid, not in the months to which it relates.
5836	Aspect
5837	Treatment
5838	Withholding tax
5839	The arrears are added to the taxable payments of the month of payment and the ordinary calculation runs. There is no re-opening of prior months and no re-computation of prior withholding. Tax Code art. 383 applies to "payments or credits to account" — payments or credits — so the trigger is the payment
5840	Statutory benefits
5841	The arrears retrospectively increase the base for the service bonus, severance savings, interest and vacation for the periods to which they relate. These must be recalculated and topped up, because the statutory benefit is measured on the salary that was actually due
5842	Overtime and premiums
5843	A retrospective salary increase raises the ordinary hour value for the affected periods, so overtime and premiums already paid must be topped up
5844	Electronic payroll XML
5845	Reported through an adjustment note (NominaIndividualDeAjuste) where a validated document must be corrected, or within the current month's ordinary document where the arrears are simply a payment of the current month
5846	Form 350
5847	The arrears withholding is declared in the month of payment. No prior return is corrected
5848	
5849	Social security is the exception that requires a prior-period correction. Contributions follow the accrual of the salary, because the contribution base is defined by the salary earned in the period. Where a retrospective increase raises the base of past months, the employer must file a correction planilla (type N) for those months, reporting paired records — an A group with the originally settled values and a C group with the corrected values — and pay the additional contributions with late-payment interest. The sum of the contribution bases and of the day counts in the C records must be greater than or equal to those in the A records, which is precisely the under-declaration case a retrospective increase produces. The correction may be filed only through the same information operator that carried the original return.
5850	Practical sequence for a retrospective salary increase.
5851	1. Compute the arrears of BASIC SALARY for each affected month.2. Recompute the ORDINARY HOUR VALUE for each affected month and top up   overtime, the night premium and the rest-day premium.   -> remember the 2026 divisor split: 220 to 14 July, 210 from 15 July.3. Recompute the STATUTORY BENEFIT bases and top up:      service bonus, severance savings, interest, vacation.4. Pay the whole arrears amount in the CURRENT month.5. TAX: add the arrears to the current month's taxable payments; run the   ordinary calculation; declare on the current month's Form 350.6. SOCIAL SECURITY: file a CORRECTION PLANILLA for each affected month,   with paired A/C records, and pay the additional contributions with   late-payment interest.7. Report the arrears in the current month's electronic payroll document,   or by adjustment note where a validated document must be corrected.
5852	Two 2026-specific retrospective triggers. Both arise from mid-year statutory changes and both produce genuine arrears if not applied on time:
5853	The rest-day premium step from 80% to 90% on 1 July 2026. An employer that continued at 80% into
5854	July owes arrears on every rest-day hour worked from 1 July.
5855	The hourly divisor change from 220 to 210 on 15 July 2026. An employer that continued at 220 owes
5856	arrears of approximately 4.8% on every premium hour from 15 July.
5857	For arrears, how are contributions and deductions computed?
5858	Item
5859	Computation
5860	Employee health 4%
5861	On the corrected contribution base of each affected month, not on the payment month. File a correction planilla and recover the employee's share from the arrears payment
5862	Employee pension 4%
5863	Same
5864	Solidarity pension fund
5865	Same. Note that arrears can retrospectively trigger the 4-minimum-wage threshold for a month in which it was not previously triggered, or move the employee into a higher subsistence band
5866	Employer pension 12%, health 8.5%, occupational risk
5867	On the corrected base of each affected month, with late-payment interest
5868	Payroll levies 4% / 2% / 3%
5869	On the corrected payroll-levy base of each affected month
5870	The exemption test
5871	Re-tested on the corrected month's earnings. Arrears can retrospectively push a month above 10 minimum wages, retrospectively losing the exemption for that month and creating a liability for employer health, the apprenticeship levy and the family welfare levy on the whole of that month's base
5872	Contribution-base ceiling
5873	Re-tested per affected month at 25 minimum wages. Arrears that would take a past month above the ceiling are capped for that month
5874	The 40% clamp
5875	Re-tested per affected month
5876	Withholding tax
5877	On the payment month, not the affected months
5878	Voluntary deductions
5879	Not recomputed retrospectively; applied in the payment month, subject to the minimum-wage floor
5880	Late-payment interest
5881	Payable on the additional contributions, computed from each original due date
5882	
5883	The retrospective exemption trap deserves emphasis. Because the Tax Code art. 114-1 test is "menos de diez (10) salarios mínimos" applied per employee, per month, on what the employee earns in that month, a retrospective increase that takes a past month's earnings from COP 17,000,000 to COP 17,600,000 loses the exemption for that entire month — creating a retrospective liability of 8.5% + 2% + 3% on the whole corrected base, roughly COP 2.4 million for that single month. An engine that applies arrears only prospectively will not detect this.

6097	The comparison makes the Colombian structuring rule concrete: a non-salary pact is a social-security and statutory-benefit optimisation, it does not reduce tax — indeed it slightly increases withholding, because the lower contributions mean less non-taxable income — and it is clamped at 40%.

6100	Module 10: Reporting, Compliance & Finalisation
6101	Purpose: To define all statutory reporting obligations and final processing rules.
6102	Scope note (dev-team filtering rule). This module gives the summary table and the mechanics. It does not reproduce field-level specification content. For layouts, code tables, validation rules, XML paths and field-by-field detail, see the Colombia Report Specifications CO-DIAN-001, CO-DIAN-002, CO-PILA-003, CO-DIAN-004, CO-DIAN-005 and CO-CESANTIAS-006 and the Statutory Integrations Register CO-SIR-INDEX-001, with the DIAN-NE configuration runbook (phases P1-P7) for the one integrable authority.
6103	10.1 Statutory Reporting
6104	Describe the country's primary digital payroll reporting system.
6105	Colombia's primary digital payroll reporting system is the Electronic Payroll Support Document (Documento Soporte de Pago de Nómina Electrónica), governed by Resolution DIAN 000227 of 2025, arts. 1.5.3.1.1 to 1.5.3.9.8 (which compile Resolution DIAN 000013 of 11 February 2021), and its 269-page Technical Annex, currently at version 1.0 with XSD version 1.0.6.
6106	Aspect
6107	Detail
6108	What it is
6109	The electronic document that constitutes the tax support for the costs and deductions arising from an employment relationship. Without it the employer cannot deduct payroll costs for income-tax purposes
6110	Who must issue
6111	Every taxpayer who deducts payroll costs, in respect of payments derived from an employment or statutory relationship, and payments to pensioners at the employer's charge
6112	Frequency
6113	Monthly, one document per employee per month, "irrespective of whether the obliged party makes payments in different or shorter periods" (art. 3). A semi-monthly payroll therefore produces one monthly XML
6114	Content
6115	Three totals: accrued payroll (devengados), deducted payroll (deducciones), and the difference (art. 2), itemised by concept
6116	Deadline
6117	Transmitted for validation within the first 10 days of the month following the payment or credit (art. 8)
6118	Channel
6119	Genuine machine-to-machine — SOAP 1.2, operation SendNominaSync, payload is the base64 encoding of a ZIP containing the XML, maximum 500 KB, UTF-8 only
6120	Authentication
6121	X.509 certificate from a certification authority accredited by the national accreditation body, XAdES-EPES signature with a claimed role of thirdparty or supplier
6122	Response
6123	Synchronous base64 ApplicationResponse carrying the validation result
6124	Unique identifier
6125	CUNE, a 96-character SHA-384 hexadecimal, computed over the document's key fields plus the software identifier and secret PIN
6126	Corrections
6127	Adjustment note (NominaIndividualDeAjuste) — a replacement or cancellation document referencing the original
6128	Version literal
6129	The Version element must carry the exact literal V1.0: Documento Soporte de Pago de Nómina Electrónica; a mismatch is a rejection (rule NIE022)
6130	Uniqueness
6131	A given document number may be transmitted only once per employee (global rule 90)
6132	Employer enablement
6133	The issuer must be enabled by the tax authority; global rule 92 rejects any document from a non-enabled issuer
6134	
6135	This is genuinely machine-to-machine — and it is the exception, not the rule, in Colombia. The country runs three different integration postures at once:
6136	System
6137	Posture
6138	Electronic payroll support document
6139	Machine-to-machine — SOAP web service, digital signature, synchronous response
6140	Form 350 monthly withholding return; Form 220; the information return
6141	Portal round-trip — prepared and submitted through the tax authority's electronic filing portal
6142	Integrated social-security contribution return
6143	Portal round-trip through a licensed private information operator. The employer never reaches the government directly for social security
6144	
6145	Deliverable: Data Disaggregation Mapping Guide describing main reporting categories
6146	The electronic payroll XML requires payroll data to be disaggregated into a fixed taxonomy of named elements. The engine cannot report a single "gross pay" figure; each concept must map to its own element. The following is the mapping guide at category level; the element-by-element specification is in CO-DIAN-001.

6232	
6233	The single most important mapping rule. The XML separates salary from non-salary at element level, with paired element names carrying an explicit S or NS suffix. The engine must therefore carry a hard salary/non-salary flag on every wage type, set at configuration time, and must not attempt to derive it at reporting time.

6320	These are engine outputs that no single artefact displays but that several artefacts consume. They must be computed and stored, not derived at reporting time.
